# Android research record, 2026-11-17

This file keeps the evidence of the research for `docs/android/PLAN.md`. Agents wrote it in one session on 2026-11-17. A script made this file from their structured results. The text is not edited by hand, except for paths and look-alike IP numbers.

- "checked" means that the agent read the source in that session. "not checked" means that the fact came from memory.
- Each phone behaviour is UNVERIFIED until a run on the owner's phone proves it.
- Web facts can change. Look at the date of each source.
- If this file and the code disagree, the code is correct.

## Browsers on Android that run extensions

### Options

| Option (Nov 2026) | How to install Adgecko | Network rules (static ~3,000 / dynamic ~30,000, priority, allowAllRequests, initiatorDomains) | Content scripts (document_start, world MAIN, all_frames, match_about_blank, match_origin_as_fallback) | Background, alarms, storage ~8 MB | Popup and options page | Maintenance and trust | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| Chrome on Googlebook / desktop-Android | Googlebook laptops only (Sept 2026). Not phones (checked). | Upstream DNR code is built for desktop Android (checked) | Not checked | Upstream alarms, storage, runtime built (checked); SW | Not checked | Google | Not for a phone |
| Edge stable | Curated Microsoft store only. | Vivaldi 8.2+ | Manage Extensions > Developer Mode > Load unpacked (folder), or CRX drag (checked). | Chromium 152-156 with upstream DNR; all limits UNVERIFIED | UNVERIFIED (full uBO MV2 ran, one report) | SW, alarms, 10 MB quota expected; UNVERIFIED | Extensions menu in address bar; options via Manage extension; UNVERIFIED | Vivaldi, maintained, stable channel | Best first prototype target, no code change |

### Implications

- Do not wait for Chrome for Android. In November 2026, Google ships extensions only in Chrome on Googlebook laptops. Phones have no path, no flag and no Load unpacked.
- Start the prototype with Vivaldi 8.2 or later on the phone. Copy the repository folder to phone storage, then use Extensions > Manage Extensions > Developer Mode > Load unpacked. This needs no code change. Vivaldi runs Chromium 152 or later, so minimum_chrome_version 137 and world MAIN are satisfied. All Adgecko behaviour there is UNVERIFIED until a run on the owner's phone.
- Make a test checklist for the phone: static rulesets load (3,059 rules); a list refresh writes up to 25,000 dynamic rules without error; MAIN-world scripts run at document_start on twitch.tv, m.youtube.com and kick.com; the cosmetic and guard scripts run in iframes and about:blank frames; the popup opens from the address-bar Extensions menu; the options page opens in a tab; the 'gecko-refresh' alarm fires after Android puts the browser in the background; the storage peak stays under the 10 MB quota.
- Turn off Vivaldi's built-in ad blocker for the test, or record it, because two blockers make the results unclear.
- Use Edge Canary as the second Chromium target. Pack a CRX on a desktop browser, then use Developer options > 'Extension install by crx'. Debug with edge://inspect over USB. Edge stable is not a path for a private build, because Microsoft controls the mobile store list.
- Firefox for Android is the most durable and trustworthy route, but it needs a Firefox variant of Adgecko: (1) add background.scripts with the 16 importScripts files plus src/general-background.js, keep service_worker for Chrome, and guard the importScripts() call; (2) add browser_specific_settings.gecko.id, gecko.strict_min_version 129.0 (Ed25519 needs 129, MAIN world needs 128), "gecko_android": {} and gecko.data_collection_permissions; (3) fit the dynamic rule budget to 5,000 (read MAX_NUMBER_OF_DYNAMIC_RULES at run time, move more list rules into packed static rulesets that Firefox guarantees up to 30,000, use up to 5,000 session rules, or use Firefox-only blocking webRequest).
- For Firefox distribution, sign the XPI on AMO as unlisted (self-distribution). This needs a Mozilla account, which is an owner action. Install it with the hidden 'Install Extension from File' menu. For development, use web-ext run -t firefox-android over adb. Check on the phone that MV3 host permissions for all sites are granted, because bugs 1820867 and 1812125 affect the Android permission UI.
- A Samsung Internet companion app gives only part of Adgecko: network blocking and basic element hiding from ABP-format lists. It cannot run the Twitch video swap, YouTube scriptlets, Kick script, or the clipboard and popup guards. Build it only if the owner must use Samsung Internet. Private use can use development mode (internet://contentblock); public release needs Samsung validation. The same API also works in Yandex Browser.
- Reject Kiwi (no security fixes since 2025) and Mises (Chromium 105). Do not use Quetta, Lemur or Yandex as a daily browser, because ownership is opaque or the trust risk is high. Lemur can refuse Adgecko if its engine is still Chromium 131.
- Repository rules apply to any port: a Firefox manifest variant or a packaging script is a new build step, and the repository has no build step today. This needs an owner decision. Do not change the frozen files src/vendor/video-swap-new.user.js and src/kick.js. Keep the manifest change and the dynamic-rule budget change behind the existing tests and the release gate.
- Mobile risks for all Chromium options: Android can stop the browser in the background, so the service worker and the 3-hour alarm can run less often; the first list download uses mobile data; the popup must work at phone width; the 8.16 MB storage peak is near the 10 MB Chromium quota.
- Watch Brave: when the brave-android-extensions flag ships (Canary first), Brave becomes a third Chromium target with unpacked and CRX installs.

### Findings

| Topic | Claim | Status | Confidence | Date | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Adgecko baseline (repository) | Adgecko 0.3.6 is MV3 with minimum_chrome_version 137 (reason: Ed25519 in WebCrypto). It has 3 static rulesets with 3,059 rules (general 2,856, ubo-youtube 4, threat 199). Code caps are MAX_LIST_RULES 25,000 and DYNAMIC_RULE_LIMIT 30,000. The service worker loads 16 files with importScripts(). Five content-script groups run in world MAIN (Twitch video swap, two YouTube scripts, Kick, clipboard and popup guards). Static rules use only block, allow and allowAllRequests with requestDomains, initiatorDomains, excludedRequestDomains, excludedInitiatorDomains, domainType, resourceTypes, excludedResourceTypes, urlFilter, one regexFilter and requestMethods. Measured storage peak is 8,155,204 bytes of the 10,485,760-byte quota. | checked | high | 2026-11-17 | manifest.json<br>src/general-background.js<br>src/rules/general-network.json<br>.planning/codebase/STACK.md<br>.planning/codebase/CONCERNS.md<br>.planning/codebase/ARCHITECTURE.md |
| Chrome for Android (phones) | Chrome for Android on phones does not run extensions. There is no flag and no 'Load unpacked'. On a phone, the Web Store button installs to desktop Chrome only. ### Open questions

- Which phone brand, model and Android version does the owner use? Is it a Samsung phone with Samsung Internet as the daily browser?
- Will the owner accept Vivaldi (or Firefox) as the daily browser on the phone?
- On Vivaldi for Android, do world MAIN, match_origin_as_fallback, 30,000 dynamic DNR rules, the action popup and options_ui open_in_tab work for Adgecko? This needs a run on the owner's phone.
- Does Vivaldi for Android keep MV3 service workers and chrome.alarms reliable when the app is in the background?
- Does Edge Canary for Android (November 2026 build) still show 'Extension install by crx', and which APIs does Microsoft limit on mobile?
- For Firefox: which dynamic-rule strategy does the owner approve (5,000 dynamic cap, more static rulesets, session rules, or Firefox-only blocking webRequest)?
- For Firefox: will the owner create a Mozilla account to sign an unlisted XPI? Which data_collection_permissions value is correct for Adgecko (probably 'none', not checked)?
- Does Firefox for Android 160 grant MV3 host permissions for all sites at install, or must the user grant them by hand?
- Do the frozen Twitch and Kick scripts and the YouTube scriptlets work in Gecko (Firefox for Android)? UNVERIFIED.
- Will one shared manifest with both background.service_worker and background.scripts pass the release gate and the tests, or must the Firefox build be a separate manifest?
- When will Brave ship the brave-android-extensions flag, and in which channel?
- What is Lemur's current Chromium version?
- What size limit and which ABP syntax subset does the Samsung Internet content blocker accept? Samsung sends these guidelines only by e-mail during validation.
- Is a custom GeckoView-based browser app with a built-in Adgecko WebExtension a better long-term route? This was not researched.

<a id="web-webview"></a>
## Android WebView as a host

### Options

| Adgecko capability (Chrome MV3 today) | WebView baseline: shouldInterceptRequest + addDocumentStartJavaScript (page world), androidx.webkit 1.18 | WebView + isolated worlds (JS_INJECTION_IN_FRAME_AND_WORLD, androidx 1.16+, newer WebView) | GeckoView (only checked facts) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Host block lists (DNR requestDomains, about 229,000 static hosts + refreshed lists) | Yes. Own host-suffix matcher in WebViewClient and ServiceWorkerClientCompat. Block with WebResourceResponse(null,null,null), which gives HTTP 404. | Same | Bundled WebExtension is possible. DNR support not checked. |
| resourceTypes (263 static rules) | Partial. main_frame is exact (isForMainFrame). Other types only by guess (Accept header, file extension). | Same | Not checked |
| initiatorDomains, domainType thirdParty | Approximate. Use the top page URL of each WebView plus Referer/Origin headers. No frame identity. | Same | Not checked |
| Threat block of main frame | Yes. isForMainFrame, plus shouldOverrideUrlLoading for navigation redirects. | Same | Not checked |
| Redirect target of a subresource | No. The callback sees only the first URL. | No | Not checked |
| WebSocket requests | No. Not routed through the callback. | No | Not checked |
| Dedicated worker requests (Twitch worker) | Yes, in WebViewClient. | Same | Not checked |
| Service worker and shared worker requests | Yes, in ServiceWorkerClientCompat. No tab identity. SW cache hits not seen (inference). | Same | Not checked |
| MAIN-world document_start scripts (Twitch, YouTube, Kick, guards) | Yes. Runs before page JS in all matching frames. Origin rules have no paths. | Yes, plus a DOCUMENT_END event | Content scripts exist; MAIN world not checked |
| ISOLATED-world scripts (cosmetic, flags, ubo-youtube-isolated, twitch-directory-ads) | Only in the page world. The page can see and change them. Needs a chrome.* shim. | Yes. Isolated world with a world-scoped message listener. | Content scripts run isolated |
| chrome.storage / chrome.runtime messages | Shim over addWebMessageListener (origin rules, sourceOrigin, isMainFrame). Page can call the object too. | Same, and the object is hidden from the page | runtime.sendNativeMessage / connectNative, privileged built-in only |
| Manifest CSS (YouTube CSS files) | No CSS injection API. Use adoptedStyleSheets from a script. | Same | Not checked |
| about:blank and opaque frames (CS-10, CS-11) | about:blank inherits the creator origin. Opaque frames need the '*' rule. | Same | Not checked |
| Popup, settings page, Allow this site, Allow this domain | Native app screens; per-WebView state | Same | Extension pages, not checked |
| List refresh (alarms + fetch) and signed feed | App code (for example WorkManager and an HTTP client) | Same | Not checked |
| Background play and PiP | Activity PiP (Android 8+, auto-enter Android 12+). Foreground service type mediaPlayback. Web PiP API and Media Session API not in WebView. | Same | Not checked |
| Google account sign-in (YouTube) | Google blocks OAuth in embedded WebViews since 2021 (not checked today) | Same | Not checked |

### Implications

- A WebView app can do most of what Adgecko does. The network part is easier than expected: Adgecko's rules are host lists (requestDomains), so one compact host-suffix matcher in Kotlin can replace DNR. A full uBlock engine (adblock-rust over JNI) is not necessary for the first prototype.
- Install the same matcher in two places: WebViewClient.shouldInterceptRequest and ServiceWorkerClientCompat.shouldInterceptRequest. Set the service-worker client before the first WebView is created.
- Keep the matcher fast and small. Calls run in parallel on Chromium pool threads. Do not copy the naive method of Privacy Browser (30-100 ms per request). Store about 230,000 or more hosts in a compact form (for example sorted 64-bit hashes or a trie) instead of a large HashSet of strings. This size estimate is not measured.
- Keep per-WebView state in the app: the current top-level page URL (from isForMainFrame requests or NavigationListener). Use it for initiatorDomains, thirdParty, 'Allow this site' and the stream-site allowAllRequests rules. Add Referer and Origin headers as a second signal.
- Accept these gaps and write them down: no resource type (use heuristics), no subresource redirect targets, no WebSocket, no blob:, no service-worker cache hits, no exact frame initiator.
- Ship Adgecko's MAIN-world files unchanged as assets. Inject them with addDocumentStartJavaScript and narrow origin rules (for example 'https://twitch.tv' and 'https://*.twitch.tv'; apex and subdomains need two rules; http and https need separate rules). The frozen files src/vendor/video-swap-new.user.js and src/kick.js can stay unchanged inside a wrapper.
- For CS-10 and CS-11 (all frames, about:blank, match_origin_as_fallback), use the '*' rule and let each script check location.hostname itself. Keep these scripts short, because each one blocks page loading in every frame.
- Port the ISOLATED-world scripts with a small chrome.storage / chrome.runtime shim over addWebMessageListener. Use the isolated-world APIs (JS_INJECTION_IN_FRAME_AND_WORLD) when the phone's WebView supports them. Else fall back to the page world and treat every message as untrusted.
- Embed a settings snapshot in the injected script string, as DuckDuckGo does. When settings change, remove the ScriptHandler and add it again. Only new navigations get the change, and the back-forward cache is flushed.
- Inject the manifest CSS files (ubo-youtube.css, youtube-ads.css) with a script that uses adoptedStyleSheets. cosmetic.js already uses this method.
- Check each WebView feature at run time (DOCUMENT_START_SCRIPT, WEB_MESSAGE_LISTENER, SERVICE_WORKER_SHOULD_INTERCEPT_REQUEST, JS_INJECTION_IN_FRAME_AND_WORLD). Follow the DuckDuckGo lesson and require WebView 126.0.6478.40 or later for message listeners. Use androidx.webkit 1.18.0 and minSdk 24 or higher; Android 12-16 is inside this range.
- Media: implement onShowCustomView for fullscreen, Activity PiP with setAutoEnterEnabled, a mediaPlayback foreground service for background audio, and an app MediaSession (the page cannot use the Media Session API in WebView). Background play on YouTube probably needs a visibility override in the page; this is not tested.
- Sign-in to a Google account in the app may not work. Plan the prototype for use without an account, and ask the owner if this is acceptable.
- No part of this research needs a specific phone brand. WebView updates come from Google Play on Samsung and other brands (from memory, not checked).
- Build order for the prototype: (1) WebView shell with the host matcher from the bundled DNR JSON; (2) threat main-frame block page; (3) YouTube and Twitch MAIN-world scripts; (4) cosmetic filtering through the shim; (5) list refresh in the background; (6) fullscreen, PiP and background audio. Mark all results UNVERIFIED until a run on the owner's phone.

### Findings

| Topic | Claim | Status | Confidence | Date | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. shouldInterceptRequest: scope | The official reference says that WebViewClient.shouldInterceptRequest is called for many URL schemes (http, https, data, file). It is not called for javascript: URLs, blob: URLs, file:///android_asset/ or file:///android_res/. For redirects, only the first URL comes to the callback, not the redirect targets. The callback runs on a thread that is not the UI thread. The Chromium source agrees: a redirected request skips the callback (request_was_redirected_). Device behaviour is UNVERIFIED on a phone. | checked | high | 2026-11-17 (reference page and Chromium main branch read today) | https://developer.android.com/reference/android/webkit/WebViewClient<br>https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/heads/main/android_webview/browser/network_service/aw_proxying_url_loader_factory.cc |
| 1. shouldInterceptRequest: request data (resource type, initiator) | WebResourceRequest gives only getUrl, getMethod, getRequestHeaders, hasGesture, isForMainFrame and isRedirect. It gives no resource type, no initiator, no frame id. Chromium knows the request destination internally, but it copies only 'is outermost main frame' into the Java object. Before the callback, WebView puts the Referer into the header map. The WebViewFeature list in androidx.webkit 1.19.0-alpha01 has no feature that adds a resource type. Thus the app must guess the type and the initiator. | checked | high | WebResourceRequest page last updated 2026-08-03; Chromium main 2026-11-17 | https://developer.android.com/reference/android/webkit/WebResourceRequest<br>https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/heads/main/android_webview/browser/network_service/aw_web_resource_request.cc<br>https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/heads/main/android_webview/browser/network_service/aw_proxying_url_loader_factory.cc<br>https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/webkit/WebViewFeature |
| 1. Resource type heuristics used in practice | Fulguris (active WebView browser) guesses the type from the request: 'Accept' contains text/html means a subframe document; the file extension gives script, stylesheet, font, image or media; isForMainFrame gives the main document. WebView sends its own X-Requested-With header (app package) as a CORS-exempt header that the callback does not see, per a Chromium source comment. Whether Sec-Fetch-Dest is in the header map is not known. | checked | medium | 2026-11-17 (Fulguris main branch, last push 2026-10-28) | https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Slion/Fulguris/main/app/src/main/java/jp/hazuki/yuzubrowser/adblock/AdBlock.kt<br>https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/heads/main/android_webview/browser/network_service/aw_proxying_url_loader_factory.cc |
| 1. How to block a request | Return a WebResourceResponse instead of null. DuckDuckGo Android returns WebResourceResponse(null, null, null). In the Chromium source, a response with no input stream becomes HTTP 404 'Not Found' with an empty body. The full constructor accepts status codes 100-299 and 400-599 only. A 3xx redirect is not supported. Fulguris instead returns neutral bodies by type (1x1 image, no-op JS, no-op HTML, silent MP3). Behaviour UNVERIFIED on a phone. | checked | high | 2026-11-17 | https://developer.android.com/reference/android/webkit/WebResourceResponse<br>https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/heads/main/components/embedder_support/android/util/android_stream_reader_url_loader.cc<br>https://raw.githubusercontent.com/duckduckgo/Android/develop/app/src/main/java/com/duckduckgo/app/browser/WebViewRequestInterceptor.kt<br>https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Slion/Fulguris/main/app/src/main/java/fulguris/adblock/AbpBlockerManager.kt |
| 1. WebSocket gap | AwContentBrowserClient proxies URLLoaderFactory requests only. It sets WebSocket cookie options, but it has no WebSocket interception. Thus ws:// and wss:// handshakes do not come to shouldInterceptRequest. This is an inference from the source. A 2016 android-webview-dev thread also names WebSockets as not observable. | checked | medium | Chromium main 2026-11-17; thread 2016 | https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/heads/main/android_webview/browser/aw_content_browser_client.cc<br>https://groups.google.com/a/chromium.org/g/android-webview-dev/c/FzajQrxaG48 |
| 1. Requests from dedicated Web Workers | Subresource requests from dedicated workers come to WebViewClient.shouldInterceptRequest. A Chromium commit of 2024-08-26 adds a test for this and enables PlzDedicatedWorker in WebView. On Chromium main, dedicated worker factories use the ancestor frame (feature UseAncestorRenderFrameForWorker is enabled by default). Thus fetch and XHR inside the Twitch player worker are visible to the app. Device behaviour UNVERIFIED. | checked | high | commit 2024-08-26; Chromium main 2026-11-17 | https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/81cae44163b4460f14a2e2c48f9523cb79ba2577<br>https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/heads/main/content/browser/worker_host/dedicated_worker_host.cc<br>https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/heads/main/third_party/blink/common/features.cc |
| 1+2. Service worker and shared worker requests | A request that has no frame goes to the service-worker client, not to WebViewClient. Apps catch these with ServiceWorkerControllerCompat.getInstance().setServiceWorkerClient(ServiceWorkerClientCompat) (feature SERVICE_WORKER_BASIC_USAGE; the callback needs SERVICE_WORKER_SHOULD_INTERCEPT_REQUEST). The docs say: set the client before any WebView is created or pages load. The callback has no WebView and no tab. DuckDuckGo uses the Origin or Referer header as the document URL. A Chromium commit of 2026-11-10 says shared-worker subresource factories also use this path and fixes a prefetch-skip bug there. | checked | high | docs updated 2026-06/07; commit 2026-11-10 | https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/webkit/ServiceWorkerControllerCompat<br>https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/webkit/ServiceWorkerClientCompat<br>https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/53b864cc243e5dcb5d7188acdfa4904641218182<br>https://raw.githubusercontent.com/duckduckgo/Android/develop/app/src/main/java/com/duckduckgo/app/browser/serviceworker/BrowserServiceWorkerClient.kt |
| 1. Service-worker cache responses | When a page's service worker answers a request from Cache Storage, no network request occurs. Thus neither callback sees it. This is an inference from the Chromium design (WebView proxies the network factory). Test on YouTube and Twitch. | not checked | medium |  |  |
| 1. Prefetch | Prefetches that the app starts through the Profile prefetch API can skip shouldInterceptRequest (feature-gated). Renderer prefetches (speculation rules) still go to the callback, per the source comment. | checked | medium | 2026-11-17 | https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/heads/main/android_webview/browser/network_service/aw_proxying_url_loader_factory.cc |

| 3. addDocumentStartJavaScript: status and rules | WebViewCompat.addDocumentStartJavaScript(webview, script, allowedOriginRules) is public since androidx.webkit 1.9.0 (2023-11-29). It has no experimental opt-in. The app must check WebViewFeature.DOCUMENT_START_SCRIPT at run time. The script runs in each frame whose origin matches, when the document begins to load, before any page JavaScript. It blocks page loading until it ends. It applies only to frames that start loading after the call. Several scripts run in the order of addition. Objects from addWebMessageListener are injected first. It returns a ScriptHandler for removal. The exact minimum WebView version is not documented. | checked | high | 2026-11-17 | https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/webkit/WebViewCompat<br>https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/webkit/WebViewFeature<br>https://developer.android.com/jetpack/androidx/releases/webkit |
| 3. addDocumentStartJavaScript: world and timing | In the Chromium source, the script runs with WebLocalFrame::ExecuteScript in the page main world, unless the app gives another world. WebView starts it from ContentRendererClient::RunScriptsAtDocumentStart, which is called from RenderFrameImpl when the document element is available. Chrome uses this same hook for extension document_start content scripts. Thus the timing should match Adgecko's MAIN-world content scripts (inference). | checked | high | Chromium main 2026-11-17 | https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/heads/main/components/js_injection/renderer/js_communication.cc<br>https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/heads/main/android_webview/renderer/aw_content_renderer_client.cc<br>https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/heads/main/content/renderer/render_frame_impl.cc |
| 3. Iframes, cross-origin iframes, about:blank, origin rules | The 1.9.0 release note says the API runs scripts in embedded iframes when they load. The match uses each frame's security origin, so cross-origin iframes get the script if their origin matches a rule. Rule format: SCHEME://HOST[:PORT], a leading '*.' for subdomains only ('https://*.example.com' does not match 'https://example.com'), IP literals, a custom scheme, or '*' for all. There is no path matching. In OriginMatcher, only '*' matches any origin; frames with an opaque origin (sandboxed, data:) thus need '*'. An about:blank frame inherits its creator's origin, so a host rule should match it (inference). | checked | medium | 2026-11-17 | https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/webkit/WebViewCompat<br>https://developer.android.com/jetpack/androidx/releases/webkit<br>https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/heads/main/components/origin_matcher/origin_matcher.cc |
| 3. Known about:blank gap | Chromium issue 40202434 says a creator page can change the JS environment of a new same-origin frame before extension document_start scripts run. ### Open questions

- Which phone and which Android System WebView version does the owner have? Does that WebView report JS_INJECTION_IN_FRAME_AND_WORLD? The first WebView version with this feature is not documented.
- Which headers does WebView 156 put in getRequestHeaders for each request type (Accept, Sec-Fetch-Dest, Origin)? This needs a test on a phone. It decides how good the resource-type guess can be.
- Does a document-start script run in a new about:blank iframe before the parent page can use the iframe (issue 40202434)? This needs a test.
- Does any Adgecko script use eval, new Function or inserted script elements that page CSP can block? A code review and a test on YouTube, Twitch and Kick can answer this.
- Does the Twitch script work on m.twitch.tv in WebView, or must the app send a desktop user agent? Does Twitch or YouTube treat the 'wv' token differently?
- Do YouTube or Twitch serve player resources from a service-worker cache that bypasses blocking?
- Does m.youtube.com keep playing in the background in WebView when the app overrides document.visibilityState? Does Google allow sign-in in the app WebView at all?
- May the vendored scripts (uBlock Origin parts, TwitchAdSolutions) be redistributed inside an APK under their licences? Is a Google Play listing wanted, or a private sideloaded APK only?
- GeckoView: does it support declarativeNetRequest and MAIN-world content scripts for a built-in extension, and what does it add to the APK size? Another research step must check this before a final engine decision.
- Is a Kotlin host matcher fast enough with the largest refreshed lists (up to 2,000,000 hosts by the current code limit)? Measure memory and time per request on the owner's phone.

<a id="web-geckoview"></a>
## GeckoView as a host

### Options

| Adgecko capability | A: Own app, plain GeckoView 160 + built-in extension | B: Own app on Android Components (reference-browser as model) + built-in extension | C: Firefox for Android + Adgecko (web-ext for tests, signed XPI to keep) | D: Fork of Fenix / Iceraven |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Install without AMO signing | Yes: installBuiltIn / ensureBuiltIn (checked) | Yes: installBuiltInWebExtension (checked) | Only temporary with web-ext over adb; to keep it, release needs a Mozilla signature | Yes: built-in |
| Background (service worker with importScripts, 17 files) | Needs an Android manifest variant: background.scripts + no-op importScripts shim (checked: service worker locked off) | Same | Same | Same |
| Static DNR (3 rulesets, 3,059 rules) | Yes (limit 30,000) | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Dynamic DNR (up to 25,000 list rules + threat rules) | Yes after the app raises extensions.dnr.max_number_of_dynamic_rules (configFilePath YAML or GeckoPreferenceController); UNVERIFIED | Same | No: default limit 5,000; release has no about:config | Yes, by pref |
| DNR speed with many rules | JS loop over every rule: not measured | Same | Same | Same |
| MAIN world scripts (CS-1, 2, 4, 5, 11) | Supported since Gecko 128 (checked); site behaviour UNVERIFIED | Same | Same | Same |
| ISOLATED cosmetic and flags scripts (CS-3, 6 to 10) | Supported; Firefox does not collapse DNR-blocked frames | Same | Same | Same |
| Guards in empty about:blank frames | Firefox does not inject there at document_start (MDN note) | Same | Same | Same |
| storage.local, storage.session, alarms | Yes; alarms fire only while the app process runs (not checked) | Same | Same | Same |
| Popup (src/popup.html) | App must show the GeckoSession from onTogglePopup, and call setTabActive | AC has extension action UI (not checked in detail) | Fenix shows it | Fenix shows it |
| Settings page (options_ui, open_in_tab) | App must open a tab in TabDelegate.onNewTab | AC tabs | Yes | Yes |
| Toolbar icon and badge | App draws them from onBrowserAction | AC toolbar (not checked) | In the Fenix menu | In the Fenix menu |
| Native messaging to the app (WorkManager refresh, native UI) | Yes (privileged built-in) | Yes | No | Yes |
| Desktop site per site (Twitch fallback) | Yes: USER_AGENT_MODE_DESKTOP | Yes | Manual switch per tab | Yes |
| Prototype effort | Low to medium: one Activity, Kotlin | Medium | Lowest (no app) | High: very large build |
| Size per ABI (arm64) | About 90 to 100 MB estimate (AAR 91.5 MB) | Similar or larger | Firefox APK 135.5 MB | About 135 MB |
| Updates | Change the Maven version every 2 weeks | Same, AC pins GeckoView | Play Store | Rebase on Fenix every 2 weeks |
| Licence with GPL-3.0 | Permitted (MPL-2.0 Section 3.3) | Permitted (AC is MPL-2.0, not checked) | Not applicable | Fenix is MPL-2.0 (not checked) |

### Implications

- GeckoView 160 can host Adgecko as a built-in MV3 extension. The extension needs no AMO signing and is privileged (checked in the engine code). Each behaviour on a phone is UNVERIFIED until a run on the owner's phone proves it.
- The Android build needs a second manifest that the Gradle build generates. Do not edit the repository root for this. Changes: add browser_specific_settings.gecko.id; replace background.service_worker with background.scripts = [a small shim that sets self.importScripts to a no-op, then the 16 modules in the importScripts order of ARCHITECTURE.md section 3, then src/general-background.js]; optional: add nativeMessaging and geckoViewAddons. The frozen files (src/vendor/video-swap-new.user.js, src/kick.js) stay unchanged.
- The dynamic DNR limit is the main blocker. The app must raise extensions.dnr.max_number_of_dynamic_rules (and the session limit, for the feed) to 30000 before Gecko loads the extension. The best method is a YAML file with prefs in the app's private folder, set with GeckoRuntimeSettings.Builder.configFilePath. Another method is to give Gecko fewer and larger rules (more than 80 domains in each rule). Gecko loops over each rule for each request, so larger rules are probably faster. Measure on the phone.
- The app must give the extension UI: an ActionDelegate that shows the popup GeckoSession (for example in a bottom sheet) and draws the icon and badge; a TabDelegate.onNewTab for the settings page and tabs.create; a call to WebExtensionController.setTabActive at each tab change, so the popup finds the active tab.
- Recommended order: Phase 0 (no app): load the unpacked Android-variant folder in Firefox for Android with 'web-ext run -t firefox-android' over adb from the owner's PC, and read 'adb logcat' for manifest and API errors. Expect DNR refresh errors above 5,000 rules there. Phase 1: a one-Activity Kotlin app with org.mozilla.geckoview:geckoview-arm64-v8a:160.0.20261104181552, a URL bar, ensureBuiltIn("resource://android/assets/adgecko/", id), the prefs YAML, the popup and options delegates. Phase 2: tabs and toolbar with Android Components 160.0, with reference-browser as a model.
- Alarms in an event page run only while the app process lives. For list refresh with the app closed, use Android WorkManager in the app and a connectNative port from the background, so the app can send a 'refresh' command.
- Firefox does not collapse frames and images that DNR blocks. Cosmetic hiding (CS-7) must cover the empty space. Firefox also does not inject scripts into empty about:blank frames at document_start, which can weaken the clipboard and popup guards in those frames.
- Twitch is the largest site risk. If the vendored Twitch script fails on m.twitch.tv, the app can open twitch.tv in USER_AGENT_MODE_DESKTOP, so the desktop code path runs.
- Build only arm64-v8a for a modern phone. Store the native libraries compressed (packaging.jniLibs.useLegacyPackaging = true) to keep the APK near 90 to 100 MB. This size is an estimate and is not built.
- GeckoView ships a new version every two weeks since 2026-09. The app must take each update, because it is a browser that gets security fixes.
- A GPL-3.0 app may link the unchanged MPL-2.0 GeckoView. Keep the MPL and LGPL notices and give a link to the exact Mozilla source revision. Do not use Firefox names or logos (from memory, not checked).
- A uBlock Origin fallback exists: the full uBO runs in Firefox for Android. Gecko 160 also keeps blocking webRequest in MV3, so a later Android-only path could use webRequest in place of large dynamic DNR rule sets.

| 1. Built-in install: API | GeckoView 160 has WebExtensionController.installBuiltIn(uri) and ensureBuiltIn(uri, id). The engine accepts only a resource://android/... folder URI that ends with '/'. Other URIs fail with the error 'Only resource://android/... URIs are allowed.'. ensureBuiltIn installs again only when the version in manifest.json differs from the installed version. During development, use installBuiltIn or increase the version. The guide says each extension needs an id (browser_specific_settings.gecko.id) and a version. | checked | high | GeckoView 160.0.20261104181552 (published 2026-11-10); javadoc 162.0a1 (2026-11-16) | https://maven.mozilla.org/maven2/org/mozilla/geckoview/geckoview-arm64-v8a/160.0.20261104181552/geckoview-arm64-v8a-160.0.20261104181552.aar (classes.jar with javap; assets/omni.ja modules/GeckoViewWebExtension.sys.mjs, validateBuiltInLocation and ensureBuiltIn)<br>https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/main/mobile/android/docs/geckoview/consumer/web-extensions.md<br>https://mozilla.github.io/geckoview/javadoc/mozilla-central/org/mozilla/geckoview/WebExtensionController.html |

ExtensionData.getIsPrivileged returns true when builtIn is true. Thus a built-in extension is privileged and can use the Android-only privileged permissions geckoViewAddons, nativeMessaging and nativeMessagingFromContent (PRIVILEGED_PERMS_ANDROID_ONLY in Extension.sys.mjs). | checked | high | GeckoView 160 (2026-11-10) | https://maven.mozilla.org/maven2/org/mozilla/geckoview/geckoview-arm64-v8a/160.0.20261104181552/geckoview-arm64-v8a-160.0.20261104181552.aar (omni.ja: modules/addons/XPIDatabase.sys.mjs, modules/addons/XPIInstall.sys.mjs installBuiltinAddon, modules/Extension.sys.mjs)<br>https://mozilla.github.io/geckoview/javadoc/mozilla-central/org/mozilla/geckoview/WebExtensionController.html |
| 1. Manifest versions and background | MV2 and MV3 both load in GeckoView. The official examples use MV2. In MV3, GeckoView does not run a background service worker: greprefs.js in 160 sets extensions.backgroundServiceWorker.enabled to false and locks it. When a manifest gives only background.service_worker, Extension.sys.mjs workerBackground returns false, and the extension has no background. The extension must use background.scripts (an event page). If both keys are present, Gecko loads background.scripts. Bug 1573659 (background service worker for MV3) is still NEW. Adgecko's manifest has only service_worker. src/general-background.js calls importScripts for 16 modules, and an event page has no importScripts. The 'self.*' globals work in an event page because self is window there. | checked | high | GeckoView 160 (2026-11-10); bug 1573659 last change 2026-11-04 | https://maven.mozilla.org/maven2/org/mozilla/geckoview/geckoview-arm64-v8a/160.0.20261104181552/geckoview-arm64-v8a-160.0.20261104181552.aar (omni.ja: arm64-v8a/greprefs.js; modules/Extension.sys.mjs workerBackground)<br>https://bugzilla.mozilla.org/show_bug.cgi?id=1573659<br>https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/main/webextensions/manifest/background.json<br>manifest.json<br>src/general-background.js |
| 1. MV3 host permissions | GeckoView sets extensions.originControls.grantByDefault to true. With this pref, Gecko grants the MV3 host_permissions and the content_scripts match origins at install (Extension.sys.mjs, installIncludesOrigins). Thus Adgecko's list hosts and content scripts need no user prompt. MDN has one note: Firefox does not inject scripts into empty iframes at document_start, also with match_about_blank. This note affects CS-10 and CS-11 (the guards in about:blank frames). The schema also accepts minimum_chrome_version. | checked | high | GeckoView 160; MDN compat data main branch read 2026-11-17 | https://maven.mozilla.org/maven2/org/mozilla/geckoview/geckoview-arm64-v8a/160.0.20261104181552/geckoview-arm64-v8a-160.0.20261104181552.aar (omni.ja: chrome/toolkit/content/extensions/schemas/manifest.json, extension_types.json)<br>https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/main/webextensions/manifest/content_scripts.json<br>https://bugzilla.mozilla.org/show_bug.cgi?id=1736575 |

The script order between ISOLATED and MAIN at document_start in Gecko is not known. The code handles both orders. | 3. Native messaging | runtime.sendNativeMessage and runtime.connectNative work only with the geckoViewAddons permission, and in GeckoView these permissions are given only to privileged (for example built-in) extensions. Content scripts also need nativeMessagingFromContent. The app registers WebExtension.setMessageDelegate(delegate, "name") for the background, or session.getWebExtensionController().setMessageDelegate(extension, delegate, "name") for content scripts. No native manifest is necessary. The name must match [A-Za-z0-9_]+(\.[A-Za-z0-9_]+)*. MessageDelegate has onMessage (returns a GeckoResult reply) and onConnect (gives a Port). The app can send to the extension only through a Port that the extension opened with connectNative. ### Open questions

- Which phone does the owner have (brand, Android version, ABI)? This research assumed arm64-v8a and Android 12 to 16.
- Does the prefs YAML (configFilePath) apply before the built-in extension's DNR store loads stored dynamic rules on a cold start? Test on the phone.
- How fast is Gecko's JS DNR matcher on the phone with 25,000 to 35,000 dynamic rules of 80 domains each, compared with fewer, larger rules?
- Do the vendored Twitch script and src/kick.js work in Gecko on the mobile sites, or must the app force desktop mode for these hosts?
- Does the ISOLATED to MAIN MessagePort handshake (src/settings/flags.js) work in Gecko's document_start order on YouTube, Kick and ordinary sites?
- Does Chrome accept background.scripts next to service_worker in one MV3 manifest? If yes, one manifest could serve both browsers. Not checked; the safe plan is a generated Android manifest.
- Owner decision: own GeckoView browser app (full Adgecko, more work) or Firefox for Android with a signed unlisted XPI (less work, 5,000 dynamic rules, no native messaging)?
- Owner decision: install by adb only, or register for Google's developer verification (limited distribution) to install the APK without a PC?
- Does Android Components 160 render MV3 action popups and badges in browser-toolbar without app code? Not checked.

<a id="web-system-wide"></a>
## Phone-wide blocking (VPN, DNS) and distribution

### Options

| Option (no root) | Ad hosts (195,069 whole domains) | Threat tier (15,865 hosts) | List refresh | Allow domain / per-app | Cosmetic, YouTube/Twitch/Kick, clipboard and pop-up guards | Beside another VPN | Private DNS conflict | Cost | Distribution |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A. Own app: local VpnService DNS sinkhole, DNS-only routes (DNS66/AdAway design) | Yes, all whole-domain entries. No path or option rules. | Yes, all apps, all resource types | On phone (periodic job), same checks as the extension | Yes / Yes (addDisallowedApplication). No per-site (initiator) allow. | No | No (one VPN slot) | Strict mode bypasses it. Off or Automatic needed (Automatic: test). | Free | Sideload: ADB, later limited distribution account. Not Google Play. |
| B. Existing FOSS app with Adgecko list URLs (AdAway VPN mode, personalDNSfilter, Blokada 5, DNS66) | Yes (hosts-format lists) | Yes, if the threat lists are added | Yes | Yes / Yes | No | No | Same as A | Free | F-Droid or GitHub |
| C. Full-tunnel firewall (RethinkDNS, NetGuard GitHub build) | Yes | Yes, also IP rules | Yes | Yes / Yes, per-app firewall | No | No (chaining only: NetGuard SOCKS5; RethinkDNS proxies, not checked) | Same as A; lockdown works better | Free; more battery than A (not measured) | F-Droid or GitHub; NetGuard Play build has no hosts blocking |
| D. Private DNS + NextDNS | Built-in lists; no bulk custom import; no published denylist cap | Built-in threat feeds (not checked) | Server side | Allowlist yes / no per-app | No | Not checked | It is the Private DNS | Free 300k queries/month; Pro 1.99 USD/month | No app |
| E. Private DNS + AdGuard DNS | Custom URL lists count toward 1K (Personal), 5K (Team), 100K (Enterprise) rules | Built-in filters | Server side | Yes / no | No | Not checked | It is the Private DNS | Prices not checked | No app |
| F. Private DNS + Control D | 10,000 custom rules; no bulk import documented | Built-in filters | Server side | Yes / no | No | Not checked | It is the Private DNS | Prices not checked | No app |
| G. Private DNS + Cloudflare Gateway (DoT hostname per location) | Max 100 lists x 1,000 = 100,000 entries; not all 195,069 | Yes, fits | By owner script through API | Yes / no | No | Not checked | It is the Private DNS | Free tier (user cap not checked) | No app |
| H. Own Cloudflare Worker DoH resolver (serverless-dns or custom) | Yes (Worker size 64 MiB) | Yes | By redeploy or KV | Yes / no | No | Not checked | Private DNS can use it only on Android 16 with DDR flags set by ADB (experimental). Else use it as upstream of A, or as Chrome custom secure DNS. | Free 100,000 requests/day, 10 ms CPU each | No app, or with A |

### Implications

- Option A (own DNS-only VpnService app) is the best base for a prototype. It holds all 195,069 whole domains and the 15,865 threat hosts on the phone, needs no account and no cloud, and copies a design that five open-source apps use.
- Prototype tunnel design: TUN address from a free TEST-NET /24 (192.0.2.1, then 198.51.100.1, then 203.0.113.1), fake DNS server addresses in that subnet, addRoute only for those /32 addresses; for IPv6 a /120 (2001:db8::/120 as in DNS66 and AdAway, or a random ULA as in RethinkDNS) only when IPv6 DNS exists; allowFamily(AF_INET) and allowFamily(AF_INET6); setBlocking(true); setMetered(false); protect() the upstream socket.
- Block response: NODATA (NOERROR plus a negative-cache SOA, as DNS66 and AdAway) or NXDOMAIN (NetGuard default, Blokada 5). Both fail fast. Use a short negative TTL so that 'Allow this domain' acts soon.
- Upstream: DoH from the app (RFC 8484, for example OkHttp DnsOverHttps as in AdAway), with fallback to the network DNS servers. A Cloudflare Worker DoH resolver (option H) is optional as upstream.
- Reuse the Adgecko list pipeline in Kotlin: the same 8 sources, only whole-domain entries, the threat compile, protected hosts, never-block-CAPTCHA, size and growth checks, Expires-based refresh with a periodic background job. Keep the domain set compact in memory (for example sorted 64-bit hashes); this is a design idea, not measured.
- Foreground service: on Android 14+ use FOREGROUND_SERVICE_TYPE_SYSTEM_EXEMPTED (allowed for VPN apps), declare FOREGROUND_SERVICE_SYSTEM_EXEMPTED, and have a fallback type as RethinkDNS does. For boot start, rely on always-on VPN, not a BOOT_COMPLETED receiver.
- The app must detect and warn about Private DNS in strict (hostname) mode and about a custom provider in Chrome secure DNS, because both bypass the sinkhole. The app cannot block apps with their own DoH or DoT.
- Do not promise 'Block connections without VPN' support with DNS-only routes. Lockdown needs a full-tunnel design (option C), which is a larger second step.
- The Android app cannot replace the browser features: cosmetic hiding, YouTube, Twitch and Kick ad handling, the clipboard guard, the pop-up guard and per-site allow need a browser that runs extensions. Another research area must cover that.
- Distribution: do not plan for Google Play (the Device and Network Abuse policy forbids blocking ads in other apps). Install with ADB (exempt from developer verification and from the 24-hour wait). Before the 2027 global rollout, register the package in a free limited distribution account. F-Droid is possible later (GPL-3.0), but download lists at run time to avoid a Non-Free Assets label for the Peter Lowe list.
- Set targetSdk 35 or 36 and minSdk 26 or higher: Android 15+ blocks targetSdk below 24, and verification covers Android 8+ devices.
- A no-code fallback for the threat tier exists: Cloudflare Gateway (option G) holds 100,000 entries on a Standard account, which is enough for the 15,865 threat hosts but not for all ad domains.
- Updating the app while its VPN runs can break network access on Android 16 (reported bug). The update procedure must stop the VPN first, or the owner must reboot after the update.

It gives each upstream server a fake alias address, calls addDnsServer(alias) and addRoute(alias, 32). It adds 2001:db8::/120 for IPv6 only when the network has IPv6 DNS servers. | VpnService.Builder API | If no address, route or DNS server of a family is added, the VPN blocks all traffic of that family, unless allowFamily() is called. If no DNS server is set, the default network DNS is used. allowBypass() lets apps bind to other networks. Apps that target API 29+ get a metered VPN by default; setMetered(false) inherits meteredness. addRoute(IpPrefix) and excludeRoute() need API 33. Only one VPN interface is active at a time. | checked | high | 2026-11-17 | https://developer.android.com/reference/android/net/VpnService.Builder |
| VPN lifecycle and one-VPN limit | Only one VPN service is active for each user or profile. A new VPN stops the active one. The app must call VpnService.prepare() each time. Always-on VPN exists from Android 7; the system then starts and stops the service. 'Block connections without VPN' blocks all non-VPN traffic. On Android 8+ the service must call startForeground(). Per-app VPN takes an allowed list or a disallowed list, not both. | checked | high | 2026-11-17 | https://developer.android.com/develop/connectivity/vpn |
| Gap: Private DNS strict mode | Private DNS in hostname (strict) mode sends DNS over TLS on port 853 to the provider, so a DNS-only sinkhole does not see the queries. AdGuard says that from Android Q, Private DNS forces apps through the system resolver and past AdGuard. | Gap: Private DNS automatic mode | In Automatic mode, Android tries DoT to the DNS server that the VPN advertises. It needs a test on the owner's phone. | not checked | medium |  |  |
| Gap: Private DNS and DoH | Since a 2022 Play system update, Android 11+ uses DNS-over-HTTP/3 in Private DNS only for Google and Cloudflare. Other hostnames use DoT on port 853. This is experimental and not documented by Google. | checked | medium | 2026-10-24 (Loophole); 2022-07 (Google blog, from search result only) | https://github.com/eyalm2000/loophole<br>https://security.googleblog.com/2022/07/<br>https://9to5google.com/2022/07/19/android-dns-over-http/ |
| Gap: Chrome secure DNS | Chrome 'automatic' secure DNS upgrades only when the system DNS server IP, or the Android Private DNS DoT hostname, matches an entry in Chrome's built-in provider list (Google, Cloudflare, Quad9, NextDNS, CleanBrowsing and others). A sinkhole VPN advertises a TEST-NET or ULA address, so Chrome finds no match and uses the system resolver (inference, not tested on a device). A custom provider that the user sets in Chrome bypasses the sinkhole. | checked | medium | 2026-11-17 | https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/main/net/dns/public/doh_provider_entry.cc<br>https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/main/net/dns/public/doh_provider_entry.h<br>https://github.com/M66B/NetGuard/blob/master/ADBLOCKING.md |
| Gap: apps with their own DoH or DoT | Apps with their own DoH or DoT bypass a DNS sinkhole. NetGuard FAQ 64 says interception is not possible because the traffic is encrypted. A partial mitigation is to route the IP addresses of known DoH resolvers into the TUN and drop that traffic, so apps fall back to the system resolver. That mitigation is a design idea and is not tested. | checked | medium | 2026-08-01 | https://github.com/M66B/NetGuard/blob/master/FAQ.md |
| Gap: always-on and lockdown | NetGuard says 'Block connections without VPN' makes Android stop sending domain names to the VPN after some time. A DNS66 issue reports no connection with always-on plus block. A user report about Control D says the same. With DNS-only routes, lockdown probably blocks all non-DNS traffic because that traffic leaves outside the VPN. Thus a DNS-only design must not promise lockdown support. Lockdown needs a full-tunnel design (NetGuard or RethinkDNS firewall mode). | checked | medium | 2026-11-17 | https://github.com/M66B/NetGuard/blob/master/ADBLOCKING.md<br>https://github.com/julian-klode/dns66/issues/464<br>https://docs.controld.com/discuss/68077d9be5e57b0010a207a7 |

### Open questions

- Which phone brand, model and Android version does the owner have? Samsung needs Auto Blocker turned off for sideloading and ADB over USB.
- In which country does the owner live? Developer verification enforcement started on 2026-09-30 only in Brazil, Indonesia, Singapore and Thailand, and only for participating stores.
- Does the owner use another VPN app (for example a commercial VPN)? Only one VPN can be active, so options A to C conflict with it.
- Does the owner use Private DNS today (Off, Automatic or a hostname) and Chrome secure DNS with a custom provider? Does Automatic mode work with a DNS-only sinkhole on the owner's Android version? This needs a test on the phone.
- Does the owner need 'Block connections without VPN' (lockdown)? If yes, the design must be full-tunnel, not DNS-only.
- Does the owner accept a cloud resolver (options D to H) for privacy and cost, or only on-phone filtering?
- Is the limited distribution account limit 20 devices (FAQ, June 2026) or 50 devices (current guide page)? This does not matter for one phone, but the conflict is open.
- Is the Android 16 VPN-update bug fixed on the owner's phone in November 2026?
- Does Play Protect show a warning for a self-signed APK installed through ADB on the owner's phone? Not checked.
- Which browser on Android will carry the browser-only features (cosmetic, YouTube, Twitch, Kick, guards)? This is outside the DNS area.

<a id="web-streaming-mobile"></a>
## YouTube, Twitch and Kick on Android

### Options

| Option (Android) | YouTube player ads | YouTube feed / Shorts sponsored | Twitch stream ads | Kick stream ads | General ad lists + hide rules | Malware hosts | Clipboard guard | Pop-up guard | Native apps (YouTube, Twitch, Kick) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A. Adgecko as a Firefox for Android extension | Probably yes. Same uBO layer and youtube.js; m.youtube.com gets fewer upstream fixes; no SSAP fix for mobile. UNVERIFIED | Yes (uAssets has mobile get_watch and Shorts rules) | Hook attaches on m.twitch.tv (emulation). Ad swap not tested. Upstream says no mobile support | kick.js targets IVS events and IMA selectors that exist in the mobile bundle. UNVERIFIED | Yes (DNR from Firefox 113) | Yes, as a second layer to Firefox's own protection (not checked) | Yes (MAIN world from Firefox 128) | Yes | No |
| B. Adgecko as an Edge for Android extension | Possible. Same Chromium MV3 code. Store and install rules not checked | Possible | Possible | Possible | Possible | Low extra value over built-in protection (not checked) | Possible | Possible | No |
| C. Chrome for Android (stable) | No (no extensions) | No | No | No | No | Built-in Safe Browsing, real time since 2024 | Enhanced Protection copied-command check; Android scope not stated | Chrome built-in pop-up blocking only (not checked) | No |
| D. Own browser app (GeckoView or WebView shell with injected scripts) | Possible, large work. WebView cannot run WebExtensions; GeckoView support not checked | Possible | Possible if worker fetch can be hooked | Possible | Possible | Possible | Possible | Possible | No |
| E. System-wide Private DNS or local-VPN filter app | No (same hosts as content) | No | No | No (server-stitched) | Domain block only, no hide rules | Yes, for all apps | No | No | Only third-party ad SDK domains, not in-stream ads |
| F. Patched native apps (fact only, not a port option) | Yes per community | Yes per community | Twitch patches exist (not checked) | Not known | n/a | n/a | n/a | n/a | Terms-of-service breach risk; sideload rules from 2027 |

### Implications

- Item 1 (YouTube): An Android port can reuse the CS-2, CS-3 and CS-4 YouTube layer, because the manifest already matches m.youtube.com and youtube.js handles /youtubei/v1/get_watch. The port must sync the two new 2026 uAssets rules for m.youtube.com (Sponsored prune in /get_watch, prefetch prune in /resolve_url). The port cannot remove ads that YouTube stitches into the stream (SSAP) on the mobile site; uAssets has no mobile SSAP fix, and the desktop fix depends on WEB client tricks. A run on the owner's phone (signed in, home network) must show if MWEB gets adPlacements or stitched ads.
- Item 2 (Twitch): m.twitch.tv is a working web player and the vendored video-swap-new hook attaches there in phone emulation. But upstream is archived, has 'no plans' for m.twitch.tv, and calls video-swap-new 'Not recommended'. The vendored copy (internal version 23) is 32 versions behind the most active fork (internal version 55, backup types popout, mobile_web, embed). Re-vendoring touches a frozen file and needs the owner's approval. The mobile site also has display-ad components (stream display ad pushdown, audio ad overlay) that a hide sheet like twitch-directory-ads.js can target. The port cannot block ads in the Twitch app.
- Item 3 (Kick): kick.js listens to IVS ad-break events and hides IMA ad chrome, including the ad-learn-more-mobile button that exists in the Kick mobile bundle. Thus kick.js can run in a mobile browser extension without design changes (UNVERIFIED). Kick in-stream ads are probably server-stitched by Amazon IVS (launched 2026-04-22), so the port can only skip by seek when the player can jump back. The port cannot block ads in the Kick app.
- Item 4 (native apps): No Adgecko port can block in-stream ads inside the YouTube, Twitch or Kick apps without root or a patched app. DNS cannot do it, and AdGuard says it cannot change YouTube app traffic. A safe feature is a hand-off: 'open this link in the protected browser' (share target or link handler). Do not ship or recommend patched apps: the YouTube Terms forbid circumvention, and the 2027 developer verification makes sideloading harder.
- Item 5 (clipboard and redirects): The clipboard guard and the pop-up guard can run in a Firefox for Android extension (MAIN world from Firefox 128). But the Android threat forms differ from desktop ClickFix: SMS-prefill scams (sms: links to many international numbers), fake 'update' pages that push APKs, PWA install plus notification and contact permission abuse, clipboard reads on focus, and back-button history traps. An Android port should add guards for sms:, tel: and intent: navigations with many recipients, a warning before an APK download from a web page, and a history-trap guard. These are design proposals, not tested.
- Item 6 (malware hosts): In Chrome, real-time Safe Browsing (since 2024) and Enhanced Protection give most of the value; the Adgecko threat tier (15,865 hosts, 3-hour refresh) adds little there. In Firefox for Android it is a second layer. The largest new value on Android is a system-wide DNS or local-VPN mode that applies the threat lists to all apps, but that mode cannot block video ads or hide elements.
- Prototype path: the fastest working prototype is a Firefox for Android build of the existing extension. Required changes: background.scripts instead of background.service_worker (Firefox does not support service_worker), the importScripts order as a scripts array, strict_min_version 128 for MAIN-world content scripts and match_origin_as_fallback, and a check of DNR features (allowAllRequests, rule limits) in Firefox. Test on a real phone with remote debugging before any claim.

| 2. video-swap-new on m.twitch.tv | The vendored video-swap-new (v1.55) was injected at document start into the phone-emulated m.twitch.tv page. It logged 'hookWorkerFetch (video-swap-new)', which shows that the worker fetch hook attached. The stream continued to play. ### Open questions

- Does YouTube send adPlacements or stitched (SSAP) ads to the MWEB client for a signed-in user on a home network? This needs a run on the owner's phone.
- Does video-swap-new swap an ad break on m.twitch.tv on a real phone? Does Twitch serve ads to m.twitch.tv in the owner's region? No ad break came in the 30-second test.
- Does kick.js skip IVS ad breaks and hide the IMA cover in Firefox for Android?
- Does Chrome's copied-command (ClickFix) check run on Chrome for Android, or only on desktop?
- Does Firefox for Android support all DNR features that Adgecko uses (allowAllRequests, the priority ranges, about 30,000 dynamic rules, session rules)? Firefox's own Safe Browsing behaviour on Android was not checked.
- Can a self-distributed (unlisted, signed) extension be installed on Firefox for Android release, or must it be listed on addons.mozilla.org?
- Does Edge for Android let a developer publish any MV3 extension, or only a curated list?
- Which browser and which phone brand does the owner use? Samsung Internet content blockers were not researched.
- Owner decision: re-vendor TwitchAdSolutions from the active fork (frozen file, needs approval)?
- Owner decision: build a separate system-wide DNS or local-VPN app for malware hosts (needs developer verification for sideloading from 2027)?

<a id="web-native-streams"></a>
## Native ad-free stream playback

### Options

| Option | Adgecko jobs covered | Effort | Risk | Maintenance | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A. Native Twitch player (Media3) with a Kotlin port of the video-swap-new playlist swap and ad-segment strip | Job 4 (Twitch), job 7 (one-tap open from share) | Medium: about 2 to 4 weeks for one developer (estimate) | Twitch ToS s.7(c)(d), s.10 vi/xi. 360p during ad breaks (fork field notes). Uses the Twitch web Client-Id. No login, so no account to lose. | High. The upstream script is archived (2026-03-05). Forks change often. Put player types, markers, GQL hash and Client-Id in a signed config. | Recommended as the prototype (Twitch-first). UNVERIFIED on the phone. |
| B. Hand Twitch links to an installed Xtra (explicit-package intent) | Job 4 (Twitch) | Small: 1 to 2 days | Xtra has the same ToS exposure. Adgecko ships no Twitch code. | None for Adgecko. Xtra is active (2026-11-01). | Good fallback and a comparison baseline. |
| C. Opt-in HTTP CONNECT proxy for the token or playlist (TTV LOL PRO style, as in Xtra) | Job 4 (Twitch), full quality during ads if the proxy works | Small to medium | Privacy: the proxy operator sees the phone's IP and the Twitch hosts. TLS stays end to end (CONNECT), so the phone's security is not weakened. Conflicts with job 8 ('no tracking') if on by default. | Depends on a third-party proxy's uptime. | Only as an opt-in owner decision. Default off. |
| D. Twitch ad-beacon spoofing (fake impression and quartile events) | None that Adgecko needs | Small | Reports ad views that did not occur. | Medium | Rejected (false ad reports). |
| E. Native Kick player from kick.com/api/v2/channels playback_url | Job 4 (Kick) | Small to medium: about 1 week | Kick ToS 1.4(a)(b)(k)(l). Cloudflare can block the API. In-stream ad marker is unknown. | Medium. Unofficial endpoint can change. | Second step, after one capture of a Kick playlist during an ad. |
| F. Embed NewPipe Extractor (GPL-3.0) for YouTube playback | Job 4 (YouTube) | Large: 3 to 6 weeks plus updates | YouTube ToS (circumvent, automated access). APK grows (Rhino, protobuf, jsoup). | Very high: 10 releases in 2026; SABR outage of about 3 months. | Not recommended. |
| G. Hand YouTube links to an installed client (NewPipe, Tubular, LibreTube, PipePipe) | Job 4 (YouTube); SponsorBlock comes with Tubular, LibreTube, PipePipe | Small: 1 to 2 days | Risk stays with the client project. | None for Adgecko. | Recommended for YouTube. |
| H. SponsorBlock client (hash-prefix API) inside the Adgecko player | Covers the README limit 'ads that are part of the video itself still play' | Small: 2 to 3 days | CC BY-NC-SA 4.0: attribution, non-commercial. Hash prefix keeps the video ID private. | Low. Stable API. | Only if Adgecko plays YouTube itself (option F). Else use a client that has it. |
| I. Link router: share-sheet target plus optional 'Open by default' domains | Job 7 (one tap), entry for A, E, G | Small: 1 to 2 days | None. The user must turn off 'Open supported links' for each official app. | Low. | Recommended. The share sheet is the reliable path. |
| J. Native chat (Twitch anonymous IRC WebSocket; Kick chat history poll) | Not an Adgecko job; feature parity | Small (Twitch) to medium (Kick) | justinfan is undocumented. Kick endpoint is unofficial. | Low to medium. | Optional, later. |
| K. Network-level blocking (DNS or local VPN) for video ads | Jobs 1 and 2 for all apps | Covered by other research | Twitch and Kick ads are stitched into the same playlists, so host blocking probably cannot remove them (not checked). | n/a | Not a solution for job 4. |
| L. Patched official apps (for example ReVanced) | n/a | n/a | Third-party re-signed copies of company apps. | n/a | Named only as a fact. Out of scope; no build steps. |

### Prototype sketch

Goal: the smallest Twitch-first native player that reuses the playlist-swap logic of src/vendor/video-swap-new.user.js (MIT, v1.55). The port is a new Kotlin file set. It does not edit the frozen file. Each behaviour below is UNVERIFIED until a run on the owner's phone.

1. Modules
- :swap-core: pure Kotlin/JVM, no Android classes. It is the port of the JS logic. It gets HTTP through a small Fetcher interface, so tests can replace the network.
- :app: Android activities, Media3 player, MediaSessionService, link router, chat.

2. Libraries (versions checked on 2026-11-17 where marked)
- androidx.media3: media3-exoplayer, media3-exoplayer-hls, media3-session, media3-ui (or media3-ui-compose), media3-datasource-okhttp: 1.11.1 (checked on Google Maven; 1.12.0-beta01 exists). Licence Apache-2.0 (not checked).
- com.squareup.okhttp3:okhttp 5.x (Xtra pins 5.5.1, checked). Licence Apache-2.0 (not checked).
- org.jetbrains.kotlinx:kotlinx-serialization-json and kotlinx-coroutines (versions not checked). Licence Apache-2.0 (not checked).
- Tests: JUnit, okhttp3 mockwebserver, androidx.media3:media3-test-utils and media3-test-utils-robolectric 1.11.1, Robolectric (versions not checked).
- No Google Play services, no analytics, no login, no WebView.

3. Manifest
- minSdk 33 (Android 13), targetSdk 36 (Android 16).
- LinkRouterActivity (exported). Filter 1: action SEND, mimeType text/plain. Filter 2: action VIEW, categories DEFAULT and BROWSABLE, schemes http and https, hosts twitch.tv, www.twitch.tv, m.twitch.tv (later kick.com, youtube.com, youtu.be). No autoVerify: the app cannot verify these domains.
- PlayerActivity: supportsPictureInPicture=true, configChanges for orientation and screenSize.
- PlaybackService extends MediaSessionService: foregroundServiceType=mediaPlayback. Permissions: INTERNET, FOREGROUND_SERVICE, FOREGROUND_SERVICE_MEDIA_PLAYBACK, POST_NOTIFICATIONS.

4. Map from video-swap-new.user.js to native code
- L23-42 declareOptions -> SwapConfig: backupPlayerTypes [autoplay(android), picture-by-picture, embed], forcedPlayerType popout, adSignifier 'stitched-ad', liveSignifier ',live', clientId, persisted-query hash. Load it from a signed config file, so that a change does not need a new APK (Adgecko job 8).
- L583-603 getAccessToken and L604-644 gqlRequest -> TwitchGql.playbackAccessToken(login, playerType, platform): POST https://gql.twitch.tv/gql with Client-Id and a random 32-character X-Device-Id per install. No Authorization and no Client-Integrity (the probe on 2026-11-17 needed neither).
- L662-680 worker fetch relay and L681-730 window fetch hook -> not needed. The app sends its own requests and sets playerType itself.
- L467-531 usher branch of the worker fetch hook -> SwapEngine.openChannel(login): main token (popout), usher v2 URL with sig and token, multivariant fetch, StreamInfo, check of the first media playlist, onFoundAd if needed.
- L249-332 onFoundAd -> SwapEngine.findBackup(): loop over backup types; token; usher; same resolution (L552-582 getStreamUrlForResolution); accept the first playlist without the ad signifier; merge low-resolution URLs into the main multivariant; keep the HEVC codec fix.
- L373-424 processM3U8 -> SwapEngine.onMediaPlaylist(url, text): while on the backup, poll the main playlist; when it is clean, emit ReloadToMain. Do not port L405-415 (download of one real ad segment for each poll) unless the owner decides to keep it.
- L333-372 stripAdSegments -> PlaylistRewriter.strip(): replace X-TV-TWITCH-AD-URL and X-TV-TWITCH-AD-CLICK-TRACKING-URL; remove each EXTINF without ',live' and its URI line; remove EXT-X-TWITCH-PREFETCH during ads. Native code removes ad segments. It does not serve the blank MP4 of L426-441.
- Ad detection: also add the Xtra markers (DATERANGE ID 'stitched-ad-', CLASS 'twitch-stitched-ad', X-TV-TWITCH-AD-* attributes, EXTINF title 'Amazon', 'Adform', 'DCM') as config entries.
- L538-551 server time and L215-234, L645-656 parsing -> M3u8 helpers (or Media3 HlsPlaylistParser).
- L425-537 worker fetch hook -> TwitchPlaylistDataSource: a Media3 DataSource that wraps OkHttpDataSource. For usher.ttvnw.net and *.playlist.ttvnw.net / video-weaver.*.hls.ttvnw.net URIs it reads the full body, gives the text to SwapEngine and returns the new bytes. Segment requests pass through unchanged.
- L810-869 reloadTwitchPlayer -> on ReloadToMain or on a swap: player.setMediaItem(sameItem) and player.prepare(). Reason: the main and backup sessions can have different media sequences, and the Media3 playlist tracker can reject a jump (UNVERIFIED). Plan B: a second ExoPlayer during the break, as Xtra does.
- L235-248 and L731-753 banners -> a small overlay: 'Ad break: backup stream (360p)'.
- L56-207 worker hooks, L754-809 React state, L870-985 page visibility hooks -> not needed.

5. Data flow
Share or tap -> LinkRouterActivity takes the first URL from EXTRA_TEXT or intent data, removes tracking parameters and finds the login -> PlayerActivity -> MediaController -> PlaybackService (ExoPlayer with HlsMediaSource.Factory(TwitchPlaylistDataSource.Factory)) -> MediaItem URI https://usher.ttvnw.net/api/v2/channel/hls/<login>.m3u8 -> DataSource asks SwapEngine for the multivariant -> ExoPlayer polls the media playlist about every 2 s -> DataSource -> SwapEngine.onMediaPlaylist -> strip or swap -> ExoPlayer. Chat (optional): OkHttp WebSocket to wss://irc-ws.chat.twitch.tv, 'CAP REQ :twitch.tv/tags twitch.tv/commands', 'NICK justinfan<number>', 'JOIN #<login>', read only.

6. Test without a phone
- JVM unit tests for :swap-core with fixture playlists: clean, ad (stitched-ad DATERANGE and 'Amazon' EXTINF), backup at 360p. Make the clean fixtures from a real capture. Make the ad fixtures by hand from the markers in the Xtra and vaft source.
- JVM integration test: MockWebServer serves fake gql.twitch.tv, usher and media playlists. The test changes the main playlist from clean to ad to clean and checks each state of SwapEngine.
- Robolectric and media3-test-utils: ExoPlayer with the custom DataSource against MockWebServer HLS with tiny TS segments. Check that the player never requests an ad segment URI.
- Emulator: Android 16 AVD (x86_64). Send a share intent with adb and read the logcat.
- Contract check: a daily script sends one token request and one usher request. It fails when the hash, the Client-Id or the response shape changes.

Commands for the owner's PC (PowerShell 5.1). PACKAGE and CHANNEL are placeholders.

```powershell
.\gradlew.bat :swap-core:test
```

```powershell
.\gradlew.bat :app:assembleDebug
```

```powershell
adb install -r .\app\build\outputs\apk\debug\app-debug.apk
```

```powershell
adb shell am start -a android.intent.action.SEND -t text/plain --es android.intent.extra.TEXT "https://www.twitch.tv/CHANNEL" -n PACKAGE/.LinkRouterActivity
```

```powershell
adb logcat -s EventLogger:V AdgeckoSwap:V
```

```powershell
adb shell pm get-app-links --user cur PACKAGE
```

### Implications

- Native Twitch playback is possible today. The anonymous token request and usher v2 worked on 2026-11-17, and Xtra proves that Media3 plays Twitch HLS. Proof on the owner's phone is still missing (UNVERIFIED).
- The video-swap method gives low quality during Twitch ad breaks. Only the 'autoplay' and 'picture-by-picture' tokens give a 360p ladder, and the maintained fork says that the Source-quality backups now carry ads too. Tell users: 'During an ad break the stream drops to 360p or pauses.'
- The frozen copy is the last upstream version (1.55) of an archived project. A native port must not stay frozen. Put the player-type list, ad markers, GQL hash and Client-Id in a signed config that the existing list-update feed can deliver.
- Port from the MIT JavaScript, not from Xtra code. Xtra is AGPL-3.0. GPL-3.0 section 13 allows a combination, but a clean port from MIT code keeps the licence simple (not legal advice).
- Do not port the fetch of one real ad segment per poll (L405-415) and do not add ad-beacon spoofing. Both make the app pretend to watch ads.
- For YouTube, hand links to an installed FOSS client (Tubular, LibreTube or PipePipe already have SponsorBlock). Embedding NewPipe Extractor would make Adgecko follow about 10 YouTube-driven releases each year and outages such as the 2026 SABR outage.
- For Kick, native playback is easy through the playback_url field. Client-side Google IMA ads probably do not appear in a native player (UNVERIFIED). The in-stream ad marker is unknown until one playlist is captured during an ad.
- The share sheet is the reliable entry point. 'Open with' needs the user to turn off 'Open supported links' for the official app first, because the official apps hold verified App Links for all three domains.
- All three ToS forbid circumvention and unauthorized access. The practical risk is IP or token blocking and frequent breakage, not account loss, because the app uses no login. A sideloaded personal APK lowers exposure but does not remove it.
- No option in this report needs root, a user CA, an Accessibility service or a change to Play Protect. The proxy option is a privacy trade-off, not a security change, and must stay opt-in.
- Checked in this session: the frozen file, the upstream copy and README, Xtra, Frosty, Kickstra and TTV LOL PRO source, NewPipe Extractor tags and code, the live Twitch token and usher probe, the live Kick API and playlist, the Twitch, YouTube and Kick terms, the SponsorBlock docs, the Android App Links docs, the assetlinks.json files, the Media3 Maven metadata and the Android 17 audio page. Not checked: Kick ad markers, official app share texts, Media3 behaviour on a playlist swap, licences of Media3/OkHttp/kotlinx, the pm user-selection syntax, OEM settings menus, and any run on the owner's phone.
- HANDOFF.md, TODO.md and CHANGELOG.md were not updated, because this task is read-only.

### Findings

| Topic | Claim | Status | Confidence | Date | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Twitch: Adgecko vendored script | The frozen file src/vendor/video-swap-new.user.js is byte-identical to the upstream master copy (version 1.55, internal version 23). The upstream repository pixeltris/TwitchAdSolutions was archived on 2026-03-05. Thus the frozen logic gets no more upstream fixes. The upstream licence is MIT. The upstream README says that video-swap-new is 'not recommended' and that vaft is better. | checked | high | 2026-11-17 | src/vendor/video-swap-new.user.js (repo HEAD 0e8dd60, 2026-11-16)<br>https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pixeltris/TwitchAdSolutions/master/video-swap-new/video-swap-new.user.js (read 2026-11-17, diff = identical)<br>https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pixeltris/TwitchAdSolutions/master/README.md (read 2026-11-17)<br>https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pixeltris/TwitchAdSolutions/master/LICENSE (read 2026-11-17) |
| Twitch: method in video-swap-new.user.js | The script hooks fetch in the Twitch player Web Worker (L425-537). For the usher request it removes parent_domains, gets the multivariant playlist and checks the first media playlist for 'stitched-ad' (L467-531). When it finds an ad, onFoundAd (L249-332) requests new PlaybackAccessTokens from https://gql.twitch.tv/gql (persisted query hash ed230aa1e33e07eebb8928504583da78a5173989fadfb1ac94be06a04f3cdbe9, Client-Id kimne78kx3ncx6brgo4mv6wki5h1ko, random 32-character X-Device-Id, L583-644). It tries playerType 'autoplay' (platform 'android'), then 'picture-by-picture', then 'embed' (platform 'web'). It builds https://usher.ttvnw.net/api/[v2/]channel/hls/<login>.m3u8 with sig and token, picks the same resolution (L552-582) and accepts the first playlist without 'stitched-ad'. It merges the low-resolution URLs into the main multivariant so that the quality menu stays (L286-311). The main token request is forced to playerType 'popout' (L705-725). stripAdSegments (L333-372) marks each EXTINF without ',live' as an ad, rewrites X-TV-TWITCH-AD-URL and X-TV-TWITCH-AD-CLICK-TRACKING-URL and removes EXT-X-TWITCH-PREFETCH during ads. Ad segment requests get a blank MP4 (L426-441). processM3U8 (L373-424) polls the main playlist and reloads the player when the main playlist is clean again. While on the backup it also downloads one real ad segment for each poll (about L405-415). | checked | high | 2026-11-17 | src/vendor/video-swap-new.user.js (read 2026-11-17) |
